{\rm cl}(V_{HI})\cap{\rm cl}(V_{HJ})=\emptyset
unless
I\subset J
or
J\subset I
, and
{\rm cl}({\widetilde{V}}_{IJ})\cap{\rm cl}({\widetilde{V}}_{HJ})=\emptyset
unless
I\subset H
or
H\subset I
.

\bullet

Theorem
5.2.6
then uses Proposition
4.1.1
to compile the ep-groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
from the étale data
(V_{J},G_{J},\rho_{IJ})
. Here each
(V_{J},G_{J})
will turn out to be a local uniformizer for
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
, and the footprint maps
\psi_{J}:V_{J}\to|{\mathcal{X}}|
are lifted to construct the functor
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
.

Although the main application is to the polyfold case, all constructions in this subsection are valid when
{\mathcal{X}}
is a general étale proper groupoid in the sense of Definition
3.1.2
with a given compact subset
S\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
that is covered by a finite family of local uniformizers. The proof is a more detailed version of the arguments in
[
Mc1
]
.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}=(X,{\bf X})
be an ep-groupoid as in Definition
2.1.1
, and fix a compact subset
S\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
.
Then there exists a finite collection of local uniformizers
(U_{i},G_{i})_{i=1,\dots,N}
as in Definition
3.2.1
, whose realizations
(|U_{i}|)_{i=1,\dots,N}
cover
S\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}|U_{i}|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
.

Proof.

Given any object
x\in X
, the isotropy group
G_{x}=\{g\in{\bf X}\,|\,s(g)=t(g)=x\}
is finite by
[
HWZ
, Proposition 7.1.12]
, and
[
HWZ
, Proposition 7.1.19]
guarantees the existence of a local uniformizer
(U_{x},G_{x},{\Gamma}_{x})
around
x
as follows:

\bullet

U_{x}\subset X
is an open neighbourhood of
x
, equipped with the natural
G_{x}
-action from
[
HWZ
, Theorem 7.1.13]
.

\bullet

{\Gamma}_{x}:(U_{x},G_{x}\times U_{x})\to(X,{\bf X})
is a functor consisting of the inclusion
U_{x}\to X
and a bijection
G_{x}\times U_{x}\to{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{x},U_{x})
, which induces a homeomorphism
\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{x}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{x}$}}\to|U_{x}|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
.

This data also forms a local uniformizer in the sense of Definition
3.2.1
since these two notions are the same by Remark
3.2.2
(ii).

Since
S
is compact and
S\subset\bigcup_{x\in\pi^{-1}(S)}|U_{x}|
,
we can choose a finite subcover
S\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}|U_{x_{i}}|
indexed by finitely many
x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}\in X
. This proves the Lemma, where we rename
U_{i}:=U_{x_{i}}
and
G_{i}:=G_{x_{i}}
.
∎

The following constructions are formulated for general étale proper groupoids. The special case of an ep-groupoid (which represents a polyfold) is obtained by working in the étale category whose spaces are M-polyfolds, and whose maps are local sc-diffeomorphisms (that is, sc-smooth maps that have local sc-smooth inverses).

Lemma 5.2.2
.

Let
(U_{i},G_{i})_{i\in A}
be a finite
collection of local uniformizers of an étale proper groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}

with footprints
(F_{i}:=|U_{i}|)_{i\in A}
, and for every
I\subset A
define

F_{I}:=\cap_{i\in I}F_{i}
.
Choose an identification
A=\{1,\dots,N\}
, which induces an order
I=\{i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\}
on each
I\subset A
.
Then the following holds.

(i)

For every nonempty
I=\{i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\}\subset A
there is a translation groupoid
(U_{I},G_{I}\times U_{I})

with footprint map
\psi_{I}:U_{I}\to F_{I}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
as follows:

(a)

The domain

\displaystyle U_{I}:=U_{i_{1}\dots i_{k}}

\displaystyle:=\bigl\{{\underline{x}}=\bigl(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},x_{i_{2}},\ldots,m_{i_{k-1}i_{k}},x_{i_{k}}\bigr)\ \big|

\displaystyle\quad\qquad x_{i_{\ell}}\in U_{i_{\ell}}\,,\,m_{i_{j}i_{\ell}}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{j}},x_{i_{\ell}}),\ |x_{i_{1}}|\in F_{I}\bigr\}

carries a natural
étale structure so that

s_{I}:U_{I}\to U_{i_{1}},(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,x_{i_{k}})\mapsto x_{i_{1}}
and

t_{I}:U_{I}\to U_{i_{k}},(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,x_{i_{k}})\mapsto x_{i_{k}}
are étale.

(b)

The finite group
G_{I}:=\prod_{i_{j}\in I}G_{i_{j}}
acts by étale maps
on
U_{I}
, with the action determined by
g_{i_{\ell}}\in G_{i_{\ell}}
acting as
47
47

47

In case
\ell=1
resp.
\ell=k
this is to be read as

\bigl(g_{i_{1}}*x_{i_{1}}\,,\,g_{i_{1}}^{-1}\circ m_{i_{1}i_{2}}\,\ldots\bigr)
resp.
\bigl(\ldots\,m_{i_{k-1}i_{k}}\circ g_{i_{k}}\,,\,g_{i_{k}}*x_{i_{k}}\bigr)
.

(5.2.1)

\displaystyle g_{i_{\ell}}*(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,x_{i_{k}})=(\ldots\,m_{i_{\ell-1}i_{\ell}}\circ g_{i_{\ell}}\,,\,g_{i_{\ell}}*x_{i_{\ell}}\,,\,g_{i_{\ell}}^{-1}\circ m_{i_{\ell}i_{\ell+1}}\,\ldots).

Moreover, for any
i\in I
the action of the subgroup
48
48

48

For one element sets
I=\{i\}
we interpret
G_{I{\smallsetminus}\{i\}}=G_{\emptyset}=\{{\rm id}\}
to be the trivial group.

G_{I{\smallsetminus}\{i\}}\subset G_{I}
on
U_{I}
is free.

(c)

The footprint map

\psi_{I}\;:\;U_{I}\;\to\;|{\mathcal{X}}|,\qquad(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},x_{i_{2}},\ldots,m_{i_{k-1}i_{k}},x_{i_{k}})\;\mapsto\;|x_{i_{1}}|=\ldots=|x_{i_{k}}|

induces a homeomorphism
|\psi_{I}|:\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{I}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{I}$}}\to F_{I}
.

(ii)

For
I\subset J
, define
U_{IJ}:=\psi_{I}^{-1}(F_{J})
.
Then there is an étale map

\rho_{IJ}:U_{J}\to U_{I}
with image
U_{IJ}
given by forgetting the
x_{j_{\ell}}
for
j_{\ell}\in J{\smallsetminus}I
and suitably composing or forgetting morphisms.
That is, for
I=J{\smallsetminus}\{j_{\ell}\}
we define
\rho_{IJ}
which forgets the object
x_{j_{\ell}}
by

\rho_{(J{\smallsetminus}\{j_{\ell}\})J}(\ldots,x_{j_{\ell-1}},m,x_{j_{\ell}},m^{\prime},x_{j_{\ell+1}},\ldots)\,:=\,(\ldots,x_{j_{\ell-1}},m\circ m^{\prime},x_{j_{\ell+1}},\ldots)

for
1<\ell<|J|
, and for
\ell=1
resp.
\ell=|J|
by

\displaystyle\rho_{(J{\smallsetminus}\{j_{1}\})J}(x_{j_{1}},m,x_{j_{2}},\ldots):=(x_{j_{2}},\ldots),

\displaystyle\rho_{(J{\smallsetminus}\{j_{|J|}\})J}(\ldots,x_{j_{|J|-1}},m,x_{j_{|J|}}):=(\ldots,x_{j_{|J|-1}}).

Then for
J{\smallsetminus}I=(j_{\ell_{1}}<\dots<j_{\ell_{k}})
we construct the map

\rho_{IJ}:=\rho_{J_{k}J_{k-1}}\circ\ldots\circ\rho_{J_{1}J_{0}}

by denoting
J_{0}:=J
and
J_{n}:=J_{n-1}{\smallsetminus}\{j_{\ell_{n}}\}
.

These maps
\rho_{IJ}

satisfy the following conditions for all
I\subset J\subset K

(a)

\rho_{II}={\rm id}_{U_{I}}
;

(b)

\rho_{IJ}:U_{J}\to U_{I}
is an étale map with closed graph that is the composite of
the free quotient
U_{J}\to\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}$}}
with a
G_{I}
-equivariant étale homeomorphism

\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}$}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}U_{IJ}\subset U_{I}
; in particular it is equivariant with respect to the projection
G_{J}\to G_{I}

and induces a proper map
\rho_{IJ}:U_{J}\to U_{IJ}\subset U_{J}
;

(c)

\psi_{J}=\psi_{I}\circ\rho_{IJ}
for all
I\subset J
;

(d)

for
H\subset I\subset J
we have
\rho_{HJ}=\rho_{HI}\circ\rho_{IJ}
.

Remark 5.2.3
.

In the following argument, we will frequently use the fact that the embeddings
{\Gamma}_{i}(g,\cdot):U_{i}\to{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{i})
that are part of the local uniformizers
(U_{i},G_{i})
yield actions of

G_{i}
and
G_{j}
on the morphism space
{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{j})
,

\displaystyle G_{i}\times{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{j})\;\to\;{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{j}),\qquad

\displaystyle(g,m)\;\mapsto\;g\circ m:={\Gamma}_{i}(g,g^{-1}*s(m))\circ m,

\displaystyle G_{j}\times{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{j})\;\to\;{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{j}),\qquad

\displaystyle(g,m)\;\mapsto\;m\circ g:=m\circ{\Gamma}_{j}(g,t(m)).

The case
i=j
results from the natural inner action of
G_{i}
on the local uniformizer
(U_{i},G_{i},{\Gamma}_{i})
in Remark
3.3.5
(ii).

{\Diamond}

Proof of Lemma
5.2.2
.

Definition of the
\mathbf{U}_{I}
:

To understand the construction of the sets
U_{I}
in (i), note that for one element sets
I=\{i\}
the domain
U_{\{i\}}=U_{i}
is naturally an étale space since it is an open subset of
X
.
For a two element set
I=\{i<j\}
the domain

U_{\{i,j\}}=:U_{ij}=\bigl\{\bigl(s(m),m,t(m)\bigr)\ \big|\ m\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{j}),\ |s(m)|\in F_{ij}\bigr\}

is naturally identified with the open subset of morphisms
{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{j})\subset{\bf X}
, and thus also carries an étale structure. This identifies the maps
s_{I},t_{I}
with the source and target maps on
{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{j})
, which are étale by the étale property of the groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}
.

For general
I=\{i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\}
we can identify the space
U_{i_{1}\dots i_{k}}:=U_{I}
with the set of
(k-1)
-tuples of composable morphisms
U_{i_{1}}\to U_{i_{2}}\to\dots\to U_{i_{k}}
in
{\mathcal{X}}
.
This in particular implies that the footprint map
\psi_{I}(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,x_{i_{k}})=|x_{i_{1}}|=\dots=|x_{i_{k}}|\in|{\mathcal{X}}|
is well defined with image
F_{I}:=\bigcap_{i\in I}|U_{i}|
.

Proof of (i):

For
k=1
the properties of local uniformizers assert that
U_{i_{1}}
is a
G_{i_{1}}
-invariant étale space with
s_{i_{1}}=t_{i_{1}}:U_{i_{1}}\to U_{i_{1}}
the identity map, and
|\psi_{i_{1}}|:\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{i_{1}}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{i_{1}}$}}\to|U_{i_{1}}|
is a homeomorphism.
Moreover,
G_{I{\smallsetminus}\{i_{1}\}}=G_{\emptyset}
is the trivial group, which does act freely.

To prove the regularity assertions in (i)(a) for a general
U_{J}=U_{j_{1}\dots j_{k}}
we may assume by induction that
U_{J^{\prime}}:=U_{j_{1}\dots j_{k-1}}
carries an étale structure such that both
s_{J^{\prime}}
and

t_{J^{\prime}}:U_{J^{\prime}}\to U_{j_{k-1}},\quad(x_{j_{1}},m_{j_{1}j_{2}},\ldots,x_{j_{k-1}})\mapsto x_{j_{k-1}}

are étale.
Then we identify

(5.2.2)

U_{J}=\bigl\{\bigl({\underline{x}},m,t(m)\bigr)\ \big|\ {\underline{x}}\in U_{J^{\prime}}\,,\,m\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{j_{k-1}},U_{j_{k}})\,,\,t_{J^{\prime}}({\underline{x}})=s(m)\bigr\}

with the fiber product
U_{J^{\prime}}\,{\vphantom{\times_{s}}}{}_{t_{J^{\prime}}}{\times_{s}}{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{j_{k-1}},U_{j_{k}})
.
This naturally carries an étale structure since both
t_{J^{\prime}}:U_{J^{\prime}}\to X

and
s:{\bf X}\to X
are étale.
A proof in the polyfold case is given in
[
HWZ
, Prop.5.2.20]
and generalizes to other étale categories.

In particular, we obtain charts from sufficiently small subsets of
{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{j_{k-1}},U_{j_{k}})\subset{\bf X}
.
On those charts, the map
t_{J}:U_{J}\to U_{j_{k}}
is identified with a restriction of the target map
t:{\bf X}\to X
, which is étale
hence also induces an étale structure.
Note for later that the forgetful map

\rho_{J^{\prime}J}:U_{J}\to U_{J^{\prime}},\quad({\underline{x}},m,t(m))\mapsto{\underline{x}}

is étale, and that it is
G_{J^{\prime}}
-equivariant and constant on
G_{j_{k}}
-orbits in
U_{J}
, so also equivariant w.r.t. the projection

\rho_{J^{\prime}J}^{G}:G_{J}\to G_{J^{\prime}}
that forgets
g_{j_{k}}
.
In these charts,
s_{J}:U_{J}\to U_{j_{1}}
is identified with
s_{J^{\prime}}\circ\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
and thus also is étale.

To prove the claims in (i)(b) about the action of
G_{J}
on
U_{J}
we may assume by induction that
G_{J^{\prime}}:=\prod_{\ell=1}^{k-1}G_{j_{\ell}}
acts as claimed on
U_{J^{\prime}}
.
Then the action of
({\underline{g}},g_{j_{k}})\in G_{J^{\prime}}\times G_{j_{k}}=G_{J}
is

({\underline{g}}^{\prime},g_{j_{k}})*(x_{j_{1}},m_{j_{1}j_{2}},\ldots,x_{j_{k}})=\bigl({\underline{g}}^{\prime}*(x_{j_{1}},m_{j_{1}j_{2}},\ldots,x_{j_{k-1}})\,,\,g_{j_{k-1}}^{-1}\circ m_{j_{k-1}j_{k}}\circ g_{j_{k}}\,,\,g_{j_{k}}*x_{j_{k}}\bigr).

Since
G_{J}
is finite, it suffices to check
that this action is étale by fixing
{\underline{g}}=({\underline{g}}^{\prime},g_{j_{k}})
and considering the map
{\underline{x}}\mapsto{\underline{g}}*{\underline{x}}
in a local chart given by the map
t_{J}({\underline{x}})=x_{k}
. In these coordinates, the action
x_{j_{k}}\mapsto g_{j_{k}}*x_{j_{k}}
is étale by the properties of the local uniformizer.
Moreover, this determines an action of
G_{J}
on
U_{J}
because

\displaystyle({\underline{g}},g_{j_{k}})*({\underline{h}},h_{j_{k}})*({\underline{x}}^{\prime},m,x_{j_{k}})

\displaystyle=({\underline{g}},g_{j_{k}})*\bigl({\underline{h}}*{\underline{x}}^{\prime}\,,\,h_{j_{k-1}}^{-1}\circ m\circ h_{j_{k}}\,,\,h_{j_{k}}*x_{j_{k}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl({\underline{g}}*{\underline{h}}*{\underline{x}}^{\prime}\,,\,g_{j_{k-1}}^{-1}\circ\bigl(h_{j_{k-1}}^{-1}\circ m\circ h_{j_{k}}\bigr)\circ g_{j_{k}}\,,\,g_{j_{k}}*h_{j_{k}}*x_{j_{k}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(({\underline{g}}{\underline{h}})*{\underline{x}}^{\prime}\,,\,(g_{j_{k-1}}h_{j_{k-1}})^{-1}\circ m\circ(g_{j_{k}}h_{j_{k}})\,,\,(g_{j_{k}}h_{j_{k}})*x_{j_{k}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(({\underline{g}},g_{j_{k}})({\underline{h}},h_{j_{k}})\bigr)*({\underline{x}}^{\prime},m,x_{j_{k}}).

Next, freedom of the action of
G_{J{\smallsetminus}\{j\}}
on
U_{J}
for
j\neq j_{k}
follows directly from freedom of
G_{J^{\prime}{\smallsetminus}\{j\}}
acting on
U_{J^{\prime}}
.
For
j=j_{k}
the action of
{\underline{g}}=(g_{j_{1}},\ldots,g_{j_{k-1}})\in G_{J{\smallsetminus}\{j_{k}\}}
on
U_{J}
is

({\underline{g}},{\rm id}_{G_{j_{k}}})*({\underline{x}}^{\prime},m,x_{j_{k}})=\bigl({\underline{g}}*{\underline{x}}^{\prime}\,,\,g_{j_{k-1}}^{-1}\circ m\,,\,x_{j_{k}}\bigr).

Here we use that fact that the action
m\mapsto g_{j_{k-1}}^{-1}\circ m
of
G_{j_{k-1}}
on
{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{j_{k-1}},U_{j_{k}})
is free. To see this, notice that otherwise there is
g\in G_{j_{k-1}},g\neq{\rm id},
and
m\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{j_{k-1}},U_{j_{k}})
such that
g\circ m=m
. In the notation of
Definition
3.2.1
(iii) we therefore have
{\Gamma}(g,s(x))={\rm id}_{s(x)}
. But by assumption

{\Gamma}:G\times U\to{\rm Mor}(U,U)
is injective, so that this is possible only if
g={\rm id}
.
So given any

({\underline{g}},{\rm id}_{G_{j_{k}}})*({\underline{x}}^{\prime},m,x_{j_{k}})=({\underline{x}}^{\prime},m,x_{j_{k}})

we deduce
g_{j_{k-1}}={\rm id}
and hence

{\underline{g}}*{\underline{x}}^{\prime}=(g_{j_{1}},\ldots,g_{j_{k-2}},{\rm id})*{\underline{x}}^{\prime}={\underline{x}}^{\prime}.

Now freedom of the action of
G_{J^{\prime}{\smallsetminus}\{j_{k-1}\}}
on
U_{J^{\prime}}
implies
{\underline{g}}={\rm id}
, which confirms the desired freedom of
G_{J{\smallsetminus}\{j_{k}\}}
acting on
U_{J}
.

To prove that
|\psi_{J}|:\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J}$}}\to F_{J}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
is a homeomorphism we may assume by induction that
\psi_{J^{\prime}}({\underline{x}}):=|x_{j_{1}}|=\ldots|x_{j_{k-1}}|
induces a homeomorphism

\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J^{\prime}}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J^{\prime}}$}}\to F_{J^{\prime}}
. Then using (
5.2.2
) we have

\psi_{J}({\underline{x}},m,t(m)):=|x_{j_{1}}|=\ldots=|x_{j_{k-1}}|=|s(m)|=|t(m)|=|x_{j_{k}}|.

This implies
\psi_{J}({\underline{x}},m,t(m))=\psi_{J^{\prime}}({\underline{x}})
so we can factor

\psi_{J}=\psi_{J^{\prime}}\circ|\rho_{J^{\prime}J}|,

where
|\rho_{J^{\prime}J}|:\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J}$}}\to\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J^{\prime}}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J^{\prime}}$}}
is induced by the forgetful map

\rho_{J^{\prime}J}:U_{J}\to U_{J}^{\prime},({\underline{x}},m,t(m))\mapsto{\underline{x}}.

As we saw in (a),
\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
is étale, thus an open and continuous map. Since it is equivariant w.r.t. the projection
\rho_{J^{\prime}J}^{G}:G_{J}\to G_{J^{\prime}}
, it induces an open and continuous map
|\rho_{J^{\prime}J}|
. The latter is also injective since for
{\underline{x}}\in U_{J^{\prime}}
the preimage

\rho_{J^{\prime}J}^{-1}({\underline{x}})=\{({\underline{x}},m,t(m))\in U_{J}\}\simeq\{m\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(t^{\prime}({\underline{x}}),U_{j_{k}})\}

is a
G_{j_{k}}
-orbit,
and we have the splitting
G_{J}=G_{J^{\prime}}\times G_{j_{k}}
.
Finally, the image of
|\psi_{J}|
is as claimed:

\displaystyle|\psi_{J}|\bigl(\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J}$}}\bigr)=\psi_{J}(U_{J})

\displaystyle=\{\psi_{J^{\prime}}({\underline{x}})\,|\,{\underline{x}}\in U_{J^{\prime}},{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(t^{\prime}({\underline{x}}),U_{j_{k}})\neq\emptyset\}

\displaystyle=\psi_{J^{\prime}}(U_{J^{\prime}})\cap\psi_{j_{k}}(U_{j_{k}})

\displaystyle=F_{J^{\prime}}\cap F_{j_{k}}\;=\;F_{J}.

Proof of (ii):

Next, we check the properties of the projections
\rho_{IJ}
.
The identity
\rho_{II}={\rm id}_{U_{I}}
, footprint compatibility
\psi_{I}\circ\rho_{IJ}=\psi_{J}:{\underline{x}}\mapsto|x_{i}|
for any
i\in I\subset J
, and cocycle condition
\rho_{HI}\circ\rho_{IJ}=\rho_{HJ}
hold directly by construction.
Equivariance of
\rho_{IJ}
w.r.t.
G
requires
\rho_{IJ}
to be constant on the orbits of
G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}
. This holds since the action of
g_{j_{\ell}}\in G_{j_{\ell}}
for
j_{\ell}\in J{\smallsetminus}I
in (
5.2.1
) yields

\rho_{IJ}(g_{j_{\ell}}*{\underline{x}})=(\ldots\,m_{j_{\ell-1}j_{\ell}}\circ g_{j_{\ell}}^{-1}\circ g_{j_{\ell}}\circ m_{j_{\ell}j_{\ell+1}}\,\ldots)=\rho_{IJ}({\underline{x}}).

This also implies
G_{I}
-equivariance of the induced map
\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}$}}\to U_{IJ}
.

To prove that
\rho_{IJ}
for
I\subset J
is étale
and induces an étale homeomorphism
\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}$}}\to U_{IJ}
, we work by induction over
|J|=k
. The statement holds for
k=1
since
\rho_{II}={\rm id}_{U_{I}}
is the
identity map on
U_{I}=U_{II}
and
G_{I{\smallsetminus}I}=\{\rm id\}
.
Given
|J|\geq 2
we have again the identity

\rho_{JJ}={\rm id}_{U_{J}}
with
G_{J{\smallsetminus}J}=\{\rm id\}
for
I=J
, and for
I\subsetneq J
can factor
\rho_{IJ}=\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}\circ\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
with
J^{\prime}=J{\smallsetminus}\{j_{i}\}
for some choice of
j_{i}\in J{\smallsetminus}I
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}:U_{J^{\prime}}\to U_{I}
is étale inducing an étale homeomorphism
\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J^{\prime}}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J^{\prime}{\smallsetminus}I}$}}\to U_{IJ^{\prime}}=\psi_{I}^{-1}(|U_{J^{\prime}}|)
, so it remains to consider the map

\rho_{J^{\prime}J}\,:\;U_{J}\;\to\;U_{J^{\prime}},\qquad(\ldots,x_{j_{i-1}},m,x_{j_{i}},m^{\prime},x_{j_{i+1}},\ldots)\;\mapsto\;(\ldots,x_{j_{i-1}},m\circ m^{\prime},x_{j_{i+1}},\ldots).

For
j_{i}=j_{k}
this is the
G_{J^{\prime}}
-equivariant étale map
from (i)(a) that is constant on
G_{j_{i}}
-orbits.
For
j_{i}\neq j_{k}
it is also étale
because it intertwines the maps
t_{J}:U_{J}\to U_{j_{k}}
and
t_{J^{\prime}}:U_{J^{\prime}}\to U_{j_{k}}
which are
étale by (i)(a); and it is also
G_{J^{\prime}}
-equivariant and constant on
G_{j_{i}}
-orbits.
This already implies that
\rho_{IJ}=\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}\circ\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
is étale.
Moreover,
\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
induces a
G_{J^{\prime}}
-equivariant
étale map
\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{j_{i}}$}}\to U_{J^{\prime}}
, which we claim is injective and thus an étale homeomorphism
to its image.
Once this is established, we may quotient by the action of
G_{J^{\prime}{\smallsetminus}I}
– which is free by (i)(b) – to see that the map

\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}$}}=\bigl(\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{j_{i}}$}}\bigr)/\,G_{J^{\prime}{\smallsetminus}I}\to\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J^{\prime}}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J^{\prime}{\smallsetminus}I}$}}

induced by
\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
is an étale homeomorphism
to its image.
Composition with
the map
\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}^{\prime}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J^{\prime}{\smallsetminus}I}$}}\to\psi_{I}^{-1}(F_{J^{\prime}})
induced by
\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}
, which is an étale homeomorphism,
shows that the map
\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}$}}\to U_{I}
induced by
\rho_{IJ}
is also an étale homeomorphism to its image. Properness of the surjective map
\rho_{IJ}:U_{J}\to\rho_{IJ}(U_{J})\subset U_{I}
then follows since it is the composition of a free finite quotient map and a homeomorphism.

To check injectivity of the map
\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle U_{J}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{j_{i}}$}}\to U_{J^{\prime}}
induced by
\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
, note that the preimage of
(\ldots,x_{j_{i-1}},m^{\prime\prime},x_{j_{i+1}},\ldots)
is given by tuples
(\ldots,x_{j_{i-1}},m,x_{j_{i}},m^{\prime},x_{j_{i+1}},\ldots)
with
m\circ m^{\prime}=m^{\prime\prime}
and
x_{j_{i}}=t(m)=s(m^{\prime})\in U_{j_{i}}
. Due to the groupoid properties,
m^{\prime\prime}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{j_{i-1}},U_{j_{i+1}})
determines
m,m^{\prime}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}
up to
m\mapsto m\circ\gamma,m^{\prime}\mapsto\gamma^{-1}\circ m^{\prime}
, and the ambiguity is exactly
\gamma\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(t(m),U_{j_{i}})\simeq G_{j_{i}}

by the properties of local uniformizers.
Finally, the image of
\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
is

\displaystyle\bigl\{(\ldots,x_{j_{i-1}},m^{\prime\prime},x_{j_{i+1}},\ldots)\in U_{J^{\prime}}\,\big|\,\exists\,m,m^{\prime}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}:m^{\prime\prime}=m\circ m^{\prime},t(m)=s(m^{\prime})\in U_{j_{i}}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{(\ldots,x_{j_{i-1}},m^{\prime\prime},x_{j_{i+1}},\ldots)\in U_{J^{\prime}}\,\big|\,|x_{j_{i-1}}|=|x_{j_{i+1}}|\in F_{j_{i}}\bigr\}\;=\;\psi_{J^{\prime}}^{-1}(F_{j_{i}}).

Here the inclusion
\subset
follows from
m\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i-1},U_{j_{i}})
,
m^{\prime}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{j_{i}},x_{i+1})
, and towards the inclusion
\supset
we know that

|x_{j_{i-1}}|\in|U_{j_{i}}|
implies existence of a morphism
m\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{j_{i-1}},U_{j_{i}})
;
so then
m^{\prime}:=m^{-1}\circ m^{\prime\prime}
gives the required decomposition.
∎

The next steps in the construction of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
are to choose a cover reduction of the covering
S\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}F_{i}
by the open subsets
F_{i}:=|U_{i}|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
and construct the corresponding reduction of the data constructed in Lemma
5.2.2
.
In the context of Theorem
2.1.2
, this covering reduction will be chosen compatible with the supports
C_{i}:={\rm supp\,}\beta_{i}\subset F_{i}
of a partition of unity
\beta_{i}:X\to[0,1]
used in the constructions of §
5.4
. We state all cover reduction properties here, though only (i)-(iii) are needed for the construction of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
. The following result applies in our setting since the realization
Y:=|{\mathcal{X}}|
is metrizable by
[
HWZ
, Thm.7.3.1]
, and metric spaces are normal by
[
Mu
, Thm. 32.2]
.

Lemma 5.2.4
.

Let
S\subset Y
be a closed subset of a normal topological space
Y
with a finite cover by open subsets
S\subset\bigcup_{i\in A}F_{i}
.
Then there is a
cover reduction

\bigl(F^{\prime}_{I}\bigr)_{I\subset A}
by open subsets
F^{\prime}_{I}\subset Y
in the following sense:

(i)

The closures are contained in the given intersections:
\overline{F^{\prime}_{I}}\,\subset\,F_{I}:=\bigcap_{i\in I}F_{i}
.

(ii)

The closures intersect, i.e.
\overline{F^{\prime}_{I}}\cap\overline{F^{\prime}_{J}}\neq\emptyset
, only if
I\subset J
or
J\subset I
.

(iii)

The open sets cover the given set
S\,\subset\,\bigcup_{I\subset A}F^{\prime}_{I}
.

If, in addition,
S\subset\bigcup_{i\in A}C_{i}
is another cover by closed subsets
C_{i}\subset F_{i}
, then the cover reduction
\bigl(F^{\prime}_{I}\bigr)_{I\subset A}
can be chosen such that

(iv)

F^{\prime}_{I}\cap C_{j}=\emptyset\quad\forall j\in A{\smallsetminus}I
.

Proof.

Since
Y
is normal, we may choose open subsets
F_{i}^{0}\sqsubset F_{i}
that still cover
S
, where the notation
A\sqsubset B
means that the closure of
A
is contained in
B
.
If closed subsets
C_{i}\subset F_{i}
are given, we more over choose the
F_{i}^{0}
so that
C_{i}\subset F_{i}^{0}
.
Now we can follow the proof of
[
MW1
, Lemma 5.3.1]
by choosing nested open sets

(5.2.3)

\displaystyle F_{i}^{0}\,\sqsubset\,G_{i}^{1}\,\sqsubset\,F_{i}^{1}\,\sqsubset\,G_{i}^{2}\,\sqsubset\,\ldots\,\sqsubset\,F_{i}^{N}=F_{i}.

Then
[
MW1
, Lemma 5.3.1]
shows that the required cover reduction is achieved by

(5.2.4)

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{I}\,:=\;\Bigl({\textstyle\bigcap_{i\in I}}G_{i}^{|I|}\Bigr)\;{\smallsetminus}\;\Bigl(\textstyle{\bigcup_{j\notin I}}\overline{F^{|I|}_{j}}\Bigr).

If closed subsets
C_{i}\subset F_{i}
were given, then for
j\notin I
this construction satisfies
C_{j}\subset F_{j}^{0}\subset F^{|I|}_{j}
and thus
F^{\prime}_{I}\cap C_{j}=\emptyset
.
∎

Lemma 5.2.5
.

Let
(U_{i},G_{i})_{i\in A}
be a finite set of local uniformizers as in Lemma
5.2.2
whose footprints
(F_{i}:=|U_{i}|)_{i\in A}
cover a compact subset
S\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
,
and let
\bigl(F^{\prime}_{I}\subset\bigcap_{i\in I}F_{i}\bigr)_{I\subset A}
be a cover reduction as in Lemma
5.2.4
.
Choose an order of the set
A
and for each
I\subset A
define
V_{I}:=U_{I}\cap\psi_{I}^{-1}(F_{I}^{\prime})
, where
U_{I}
is as in Lemma
5.2.2
. Then
(V_{I},G_{I},\rho_{IJ})_{I\subset A}

forms étale data of type
V
in the sense of §
4.1
that is compatible with the footprint maps
\psi_{I}
as follows:

\bullet

For each
I\subset A
the map

\psi_{I}|_{V_{I}}:V_{I}\to|{\mathcal{X}}|
induces a homeomorphism
|\psi_{I}|:\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle V_{I}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{I}$}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}F_{I}^{\prime}
.

\bullet

\psi_{J}\big|_{{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}}=\psi_{I}\circ\rho_{IJ}
.

Proof.

We must check that the properties
(a), (b) and (c) listed in §
4.1
are satisfied.
Note that (a) and (b) hold by construction.
To check (c),
for
I\subset J
we define

{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}:=\psi_{J}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{I}\cap F^{\prime}_{J})\subset V_{J},\quad V_{IJ}:=\psi_{I}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{I}\cap F^{\prime}_{J})\subset V_{I}.

Then,
\rho_{IJ}
restricts to give a map
{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}\to V_{IJ}
because
\psi_{J}=\psi_{I}\circ\rho_{IJ}:U_{J}\to|{\mathcal{X}}|
. Further, the other properties listed in Lemma
5.2.2
(ii) readily imply that, for each
I\subset J
,

{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}\subset V_{J}
is a
G_{J}
-invariant open subset on which
G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}
acts freely, and
\rho_{IJ}:{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}\to V_{I}
is a composite
{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}\to\text{\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}\!$}/\lower 3.44444pt\hbox{$\!\scriptstyle G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}$}}\to V_{I}
of the quotient map with an injective étale map with open image
V_{IJ}:=\rho_{IJ}({\widetilde{V}}_{IJ})\subset V_{I}
. Thus the identity and equivariance properties in §
4.1
(c) hold.
To check the closed graph property, consider a sequence
(x_{k},y_{k})=(x_{k},\rho_{IJ}(x_{k}))\in{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}\times V_{IJ}\subset V_{J}\times V_{I}
with limit
(x_{\infty},y_{\infty})\in V_{J}\times V_{I}
. Then
|x_{k}|=|y_{k}|\in F_{J}^{\prime}\cap F_{I}^{\prime}
so that
|x_{\infty}|=|y_{\infty}|
. But
|x_{\infty}|\in|V_{J}|=F_{J}^{\prime}
and

|y_{\infty}|\in|V_{I}|=F_{I}^{\prime}
. Therefore
|x_{\infty}|,|y_{\infty}|
both lie in
F_{J}^{\prime}\cap F_{I}^{\prime}
which implies that
x_{\infty}\in{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ},y_{\infty}\in V_{IJ}
and
(x_{\infty},y_{\infty})\in{\rm graph}\rho_{IJ}
, as required.

The next step is to check that
{\widetilde{V}}_{HJ}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}=\rho_{IJ}^{-1}({\widetilde{V}}_{HI}\cap V_{IJ})
when
H\subset I\subset J
.
To prove this, we argue by induction as in the proof of Lemma
5.2.2
(i), assuming this holds when
J
is replaced by
J^{\prime}=J-\{j_{i}\}
, where
j_{i}\in J{\smallsetminus}I
.
We first compute the image
V_{IJ}
in
V_{I}
of the étale homeomorphism induced by
\rho_{IJ}=\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}\circ\rho_{J^{\prime}J}
on
\scriptstyle V_{J}\!
/
\!\scriptstyle G_{J{\smallsetminus}I}
by
combining the identities
\rho_{J^{\prime}J}(V_{J})=\psi_{J^{\prime}}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{j_{i}})
with
{\rm im\,}\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}=\psi_{I}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}})
and
\psi_{J^{\prime}}^{-1}=\rho_{I{J^{\prime}}}^{-1}\circ\psi_{I}^{-1}
to obtain

\displaystyle V_{IJ}:\;=\;\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}\bigl(\rho_{J^{\prime}J}(V_{J})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\;\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}\bigl(\psi_{J^{\prime}}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{j_{i}})\bigr)\;=\;{\rm im\,}\rho_{IJ^{\prime}}\cap\psi_{I}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{j_{i}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\;\psi_{I}^{-1}\bigl(F^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}\cap F^{\prime}_{j_{i}}\bigr)\;=\;\psi_{I}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{J})\;=\;\psi_{I}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{J}\cap F^{\prime}_{I}).

Therefore

\displaystyle\rho_{IJ}^{-1}({\widetilde{V}}_{HI}\cap V_{IJ})

\displaystyle=\rho_{IJ}^{-1}(\psi_{I}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{H})\cap\psi_{I}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{J}))

\displaystyle=\psi_{J}^{-1}(F^{\prime}_{H}\cap F^{\prime}_{J}\cap F^{\prime}_{I})={\widetilde{V}}_{HJ}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}.

It follows that the domain of the composite

\rho_{HI}\circ\rho_{IJ}
, when restricted to the sets
V_{\bullet}\subset U_{\bullet}
, is precisely
{\widetilde{V}}_{HJ}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}
. Hence the identity

\rho_{HJ}|_{{\widetilde{V}}_{HJ}\cap{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ}}=\rho_{HI}\circ\rho_{IJ}

holds since
\rho_{HI}\circ\rho_{IJ}=\rho_{HJ}:U_{J}\to U_{H}
. Thus the composition property holds.

Further, the separation conditions follow from property (ii) in Lemma
5.2.4
because

-

if
H\subset I,J
then
{\rm cl}(V_{HI}),{\rm cl}(V_{HJ})\subset V_{H}
and
\psi_{H}({\rm cl}(V_{HI})\cap{\rm cl}(V_{HJ}))=\overline{F^{\prime}_{H}}\cap\overline{F^{\prime}_{I}}\cap\overline{F^{\prime}_{J}}
,

-

if
H,I\subset J
, then
{\rm cl}({\widetilde{V}}_{HI}),{\rm cl}({\widetilde{V}}_{HJ})\subset V_{J}
and

\psi_{J}({\rm cl}({\widetilde{V}}_{HI})\cap{\rm cl}({\widetilde{V}}_{HJ}))=\overline{F^{\prime}_{H}}\cap\overline{F^{\prime}_{I}}\cap\overline{F_{J}^{\prime}}
.

This completes the proof that the tuples
(V_{I},G_{I},\rho_{IJ})_{I\subset A}
form étale data of type
V
.

Finally, the compatibility of this data with the footprint maps follows from the properties (i)(c) and (ii)(c) in Lemma
5.2.2
.
∎

We are now ready to prove part (ii) of Theorem
2.1.2
.

Theorem 5.2.6
.

Let
(U_{i},G_{i})_{i\in A}
be a finite set of local uniformizers as in Lemma
5.2.2
whose footprints cover a given compact subset
S\subset\bigcup_{i\in A}|U_{i}|
, let
\bigl(F^{\prime}_{I}\subset\bigcap_{i\in I}|U_{i}|\bigr)_{I\subset A}
be a cover reduction as in Lemma
5.2.4
, and choose an order
A=\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Then the étale groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
constructed by Proposition
4.1.1
from the étale data in Lemma
5.2.5
has the following properties with
V:=\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{I\subset A}\psi_{I}(V_{I})\bigr)=\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{I\subset A}F^{\prime}_{I}\bigr)\subset X
.

(i)

The étale category

{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}=(X_{\mathcal{V}},{\bf X}_{\mathcal{V}})
is covered by the local uniformizers
(V_{I},G_{I})_{I\subset A}
, and is proper.
In detail, it is given by

\displaystyle X_{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\bigsqcup_{I\subset A}V_{I}=\textstyle\bigsqcup_{I\subset A}\bigl\{(I,{\underline{x}})\,\big|\,{\underline{x}}=(x_{i_{1}},\ldots)\in V_{I}\bigr\},

\displaystyle{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}(V_{I},V_{J})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\bigl\{\bigl(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}\bigr)\phantom{{}^{-1}}\,\big|\,{\underline{x}}\in{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ},{\underline{g}}\in G_{I}\bigr\}&;I\subset J\\
\bigl\{\bigl(J,I,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}\bigr)^{-1}\,\big|\,{\underline{x}}\in{\widetilde{V}}_{JI},{\underline{g}}\in G_{J}\bigr\}&;J\subset I\\
\ \emptyset&;\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

with the structure maps

\displaystyle s(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})=\bigl(I,{\underline{g}}^{-1}*\rho_{IJ}({\underline{x}})\bigr),\quad t(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})=\bigl(J,{\underline{x}}\bigr),\qquad\mbox{ for }I\subseteq J

\displaystyle{\rm id}_{I,{\underline{x}}}=(I,I,{\underline{x}},\underline{{\rm id}}),\qquad(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})^{-1}=(J,I,\,{\underline{g}}^{-1}*{\underline{x}},\,{\underline{g}}^{-1}),\qquad\mbox{ for }I\supseteq J

and composition given as in Proposition
4.1.1
.

(ii)

There is an étale functor
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}

given by

\displaystyle X_{\mathcal{V}}\to X,\qquad\bigl(I,(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,x_{i_{k}})\bigr)\mapsto x_{i_{1}},

\displaystyle{\bf X}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\bf X},\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

\bigl(I=\{i_{1}<\cdot\cdot\},J=\{j_{1}<\cdot\cdot\},{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}\bigr)\;\mapsto\;\begin{cases}m_{i_{1}i_{2}}\circ\ldots\circ m_{i_{\ell-1}i_{\ell}}&;\mbox{if }i_{1}<j_{1}=i_{\ell},\\
g_{i_{1}}\circ{\rm id}_{x_{i_{1}}}&;\mbox{if }i_{1}=j_{1},\\
g_{j_{\ell}}\circ\bigl(m_{j_{1}j_{2}}\circ\ldots\circ m_{j_{\ell-1}j_{\ell}}\bigr)^{-1}&;\mbox{if }j_{1}<i_{1}=j_{\ell}.\end{cases}

This is globally finite-to-one on both objects and morphisms, and lifts the footprint maps
\psi_{I}|_{V_{I}}
of Lemma
5.2.5
in the sense that

(5.2.5)

\pi_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\psi|_{V_{I}}=\psi_{I}|_{V_{I}}:V_{I}\to F^{\prime}_{I}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|\qquad\text{is surjective for all}\quad I\subset A.

Restricting the target to be the full subcategory
{\mathcal{X}}|_{V}
of
{\mathcal{X}}
with objects
V
, the functor
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}|_{V}
is an equivalence in the sense of
[
HWZ
, Def.10.1.1]
, that is
\psi
restricts to bijections
{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}({\underline{x}},{\underline{x}}^{\prime})\to{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(\psi({\underline{x}}),\psi({\underline{x}}^{\prime}))
for all
{\underline{x}},{\underline{x}}^{\prime}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}
,
and induces a homeomorphism

|\psi|:|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|\to|V|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|.

(iii)

{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
is equipped with an inner action of
G:=\prod_{i\in A}G_{i}
given by
{\underline{g}}*(I,{\underline{x}})=(I,{\underline{g}}|_{I}*{\underline{x}})
and

{\alpha}:{\rm Obj}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}\times G\to{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}
,
((I,{\underline{x}}),{\underline{g}})\mapsto(I,I,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}|_{I})
with
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{N})|_{I}:=(g_{i_{1}},\ldots,g_{i_{|I|}})
as in Proposition
4.1.1
(i).
Moreover,
\psi
is locally
G
-equivariant in the following sense: For
I=\{i_{1}<\cdot\cdot\}
we have
\psi(V_{I})\subset U_{i_{1}}\subset{\rm Obj}_{{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\psi:V_{I}\to U_{i_{1}}
is equivariant with respect to the projection
G\to G_{i_{1}}
to the isotropy group acting on the local uniformizer
U_{i_{1}}
.

(iv)

{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}
is the étale (but usually not proper) groupoid given by Proposition
4.1.2
(iii).
In detail, this groupoid is given by the structure maps of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
as in Theorem
5.2.6
restricted to

\displaystyle{\rm Obj}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}}

\displaystyle=\ \textstyle{\rm Obj}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}\ =\ \bigsqcup_{I\subset A}V_{I},

\displaystyle{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}}(V_{I},V_{J})

\displaystyle=\ \begin{cases}\bigl\{(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\phantom{{}^{-1}}\,\big|\,{\underline{x}}\in{\widetilde{V}}_{IJ},{\underline{g}}\in G_{I{\smallsetminus}H_{{\underline{x}}}}\bigr\}&;I\subset J\\
\bigl\{(J,I,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})^{-1}\,\big|\,{\underline{x}}\in{\widetilde{V}}_{JI},{\underline{g}}\in G_{J{\smallsetminus}H_{{\underline{x}}}}\bigr\}&;J\subset I\\
\ \emptyset&;\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

where we denote
49
49

49

See Remark
4.2.2
(ii).

H_{\underline{x}}:=\min\{H\,|\,{\underline{x}}\in{\widetilde{V}}_{HJ}\}
.
Further,
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}
is equipped with an action of
G
so that the functor
{\iota}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
given by inclusion is
G
-equivariant in the sense of Definition
3.3.1
and induces a homeomorphism
|{\iota}|:|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}|\,/G\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|
.

Proof.

We first prove (ii).
The definition of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
implies that each object and morphism in
{\mathcal{X}}
has a finite number of preimages. Hence
\psi
is finite-to-one.
In order to establish the other properties of
\psi
, it is useful to consider the subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}
of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
that has the same objects as
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
, but has morphisms from
V_{I}
to
V_{J}
only if
I\subset J
.
Thus

\displaystyle{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}}(V_{I},V_{J})

\displaystyle={\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}(V_{I},V_{J}),\quad\mbox{ if }I\subset J,

\displaystyle=\emptyset\quad\mbox{ otherwise}

To see that
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
is a well defined functor, it will suffice to show that the natural lifts of the footprint maps

\psi_{J}:V_{J}\to F^{\prime}_{J}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|

fit together to give maps
\psi:X_{\mathcal{V}}\to X
and
\psi:{\bf X}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\bf X}

whose restriction to the subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}
is a functor that is
compatible with inverses. For then we may apply Lemma
4.2.7
to conclude
that
\psi
must coincide with the unique functorial extension of
\psi|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}}
.

Consider the restriction of
\psi
to
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}
. Let us denote

I=\{i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots\},\;\;J=\{j_{1}<j_{2}<\cdot\},\;\;{\underline{g}}=(g_{i_{1}},g_{i_{2}},\cdots)\in G_{I}.

To prove that
\psi:{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
is a functor we must first check that
the given formulas are compatible with source and target map.
Suppose first that
j_{1}:=i_{\ell}
for some
\ell>1
so that

J\subset I
. Then
(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\in{\rm Mor}(V_{I},V_{J})
has
{\underline{g}}=(g_{j_{1}},\cdots)\in G_{J}

so that
g_{i_{1}}={\rm id}
. Hence

\psi\bigl(s(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\bigr)=\psi(I,{\underline{g}}^{-1}*{\underline{x}})=x_{i_{1}},\quad\psi\bigl(t(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\bigr)=\psi(J,{\underline{x}})=x_{i_{\ell}}=x_{j_{1}}.

Thus,
\psi((I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}))=m_{i_{1}i_{2}}\circ\cdots\circ m_{i_{\ell-1}i_{\ell}}\in{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(x_{i_{1}},x_{i_{\ell}})
has the same source and target.
If
i_{1}=j_{1}
this is the source and target of
\psi\bigl((I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\bigr)=g_{i_{1}}\circ{\rm id}_{x_{1}}
.
Therefore the formulas are compatible with source and target maps if
J\subset I
or if
i_{1}=j_{1}
. Finally, note that if
i_{1}=j_{\ell}
for
\ell>1
then
I\subset J
so that
{\underline{x}}\in V_{J}
, and we have

\displaystyle(s\times t)\,\bigl(\psi(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=(s\times t)\,\bigl(g_{j_{\ell}}\circ(m_{j_{\ell-1}j_{\ell}})^{-1}\cdots\circ(m_{j_{1}j_{2}})^{-1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(g_{j_{\ell}}^{-1}*x_{j_{\ell}},\ x_{j_{1}}\bigr)=\bigl(g_{i_{1}}^{-1}*x_{i_{1}},\ x_{j_{1}}\bigr)

as required.

Now consider the composition rule for
I\subset J\subset K
, where we write
K:=\{k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots\}
so that

k_{1}\leq j_{1}:=k_{r}\leq i_{1}=j_{\ell}=k_{s}
. We must show that

\psi(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\circ\psi(J,K,{\underline{y}},{\underline{h}})\ =\ \psi(I,K,{\underline{y}},{\underline{h}}|_{I}{\underline{g}}),

when
{\underline{x}}\in V_{J},\ {\underline{y}}\in V_{K},{\underline{g}}\in G_{I},\ {\underline{h}}\in G_{J}
.
Since this is true if either
k_{1}=j_{1}
or
j_{1}=i_{1}
, we will assume that

k_{1}<j_{1}:=k_{r}<i_{1}=j_{\ell}=k_{s}
.
Since
J\subset K
, for any
j_{a}\in J
there is
p<q
so that
j_{a}=k_{p}<j_{a+1}=k_{q}
and we may write
50
50

50

Here the superscripts
J,K
indicate that the entries of
m,n
are indexed by pairs of elements from the sets
J,K
respectively. Thus
m^{J}_{ab}
is the composite
m_{j_{p}j_{p+1}}\circ m_{j_{p+1}j_{p+2}}\circ\dots\circ m_{j_{q-1}j_{q}}
where
a=j_{p}<b=j_{q}
.

(5.2.6)

\displaystyle{\underline{x}}=(x_{j_{1}},m_{j_{1}j_{2}},\dots)\in V_{J},\quad{\underline{y}}:=(y_{k_{1}},n_{k_{1}k_{2}},\dots)\in V_{K},

\displaystyle m^{J}_{j_{1}j_{\ell}}:=m_{j_{1}j_{2}}\circ\dots\circ m_{j_{\ell-1}j_{\ell}},\quad n^{K}_{j_{a}j_{a+1}}:=n^{K}_{k_{p}k_{q}}=n_{k_{p}k_{p+1}}\circ\dots\circ n_{k_{q-1}k_{q}}.

Then the identity
{\underline{x}}={\underline{h}}^{-1}*\rho_{JK}({\underline{y}})
gives

(x_{j_{1}},m_{j_{1}j_{2}},x_{j_{2}}\dots)=(h_{j_{1}}^{-1}*y_{j_{1}},h_{j_{1}}\circ n^{K}_{j_{1}j_{2}}\circ h_{j_{2}}^{-1},h_{j_{2}}^{-1}*y_{j_{2}},\ \cdots),

and more generally for each
a
and
p<t
we have

(5.2.7)

\displaystyle m_{j_{a}j_{a+1}}=h_{j_{a}}\circ n^{K}_{j_{a}j_{a+1}}\circ h_{j_{a+1}}^{-1},\quad m^{J}_{k_{p}k_{t}}=h_{k_{p}}\circ n^{K}_{k_{p}k_{t}}\circ h_{k_{t}}^{-1}

Further, since
k_{1}<j_{1}:=k_{r}<i_{1}=j_{\ell}=k_{s}
,

\displaystyle\psi\bigl((I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\bigr)=g_{j_{\ell}}\circ(m^{J}_{j_{1}j_{\ell}})^{-1}=g_{k_{s}}\circ(m^{J}_{k_{r}k_{s}})^{-1},

\displaystyle\psi\bigl((J,K,{\underline{y}},{\underline{h}})\bigr)=h_{k_{r}}\circ(n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{r}})^{-1},\quad\psi\bigl((I,K,{\underline{y}},{\underline{h}}|_{I}{\underline{g}})\bigr)=h_{k_{s}}g_{k_{s}}\circ(n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{s}})^{-1}

Therefore, we need to show

(5.2.8)

\displaystyle g_{k_{s}}\circ(m^{J}_{k_{r}k_{s}})^{-1}\circ h_{k_{r}}\circ(n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{r}})^{-1}=h_{k_{s}}g_{k_{s}}\circ(n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{s}})^{-1}.

But
h_{k_{s}}g_{k_{s}}=g_{k_{s}}\circ h_{k_{s}}

because of the categorical composition convention explained in
(
3.2.1
).
Therefore (
5.2.8
) follows from (
5.2.7
).

Hence the restriction of
\psi
to
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}
preserves its structure maps and so defines a functor

{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
. Now let us check the compatibility of
\psi
with the inverse operation

(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\mapsto(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})^{-1}:=(J,I,{\underline{g}}^{-1}*{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}^{-1}).

If
I\subset J
with
i_{1}=j_{\ell}
, then
{\underline{x}}\in V_{J},{\underline{g}}\in G_{I}
and

g_{j_{a}}={\rm id}
for
a<\ell
so that we have

\displaystyle\psi((I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}))

\displaystyle=g_{j_{\ell}}\circ(m_{j_{\ell-1}j_{\ell}})^{-1}\cdots\circ(m_{j_{1}j_{2}})^{-1}=g_{j_{\ell}}\circ(m^{J}_{j_{1}j_{\ell}})^{-1},

\displaystyle\psi(J,I,{\underline{g}}^{-1}*{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}^{-1})

\displaystyle=m_{j_{1}j_{2}}\cdots\circ m_{j_{\ell-1}j_{\ell}}\circ g_{j_{\ell}}^{-1}=m^{J}_{j_{1}j_{\ell}}\circ g_{j_{\ell}}^{-1}.

Hence
\psi
preserves the inverse operation.
Therefore
\psi
is a functor
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
by
Lemma
4.2.7
.

Next, note that the functor
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
is étale on the object space since on each component
V_{I}\subset{\rm Obj}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}
it is given by the restriction of the étale source map
s_{I}
from Lemma
5.2.2
(i)(a).
On morphisms, the functor is étale because it consists of étale actions of the finite isotropy groups by Remark
5.2.3
,
and the identity map as well as composition of morphisms in
{\mathcal{X}}
are étale since
{\mathcal{X}}
is.
Moreover, given any
(I,{\underline{x}}),(I^{\prime},{\underline{y}})\in{\rm Obj}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}
, we need to check that
\psi
restricts to a bijection
{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}\bigl((I,{\underline{x}}),(I^{\prime},{\underline{y}})\bigr)\to{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{1},y_{1})
.
In case
I\subset I^{\prime}
this is exactly the content of Lemma
5.2.7
below, and composition with the (bijective and
\psi
-compatible) inverse maps in
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
{\mathcal{X}}
establishes the bijection for the case
I^{\prime}\subset I
.

We now show that the induced map

(5.2.9)

\displaystyle|\psi|:|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|\overset{\simeq}{\to}F^{\prime}:={\textstyle\bigcup_{I\subset A}F^{\prime}_{I}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|}

is a homeomorphism between the realizations.
This map is surjective because
|\psi(V_{I})|
is the image
\psi_{I}(V_{I})=F^{\prime}_{I}
of the footprint map in Lemma
5.2.5
. To check that it is injective recall that
\psi_{I},\psi_{I^{\prime}}
have disjoint images unless
I\subset I^{\prime}
or
I^{\prime}\subset I
.
Now for w.l.o.g.
I\subset I^{\prime}
with
|x_{i_{1}}|=|\psi(I,{\underline{x}})|=|\psi(I^{\prime},{\underline{x}}^{\prime})|=|x^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}|
the equivalence
x_{i_{1}}\sim_{\mathcal{X}}x^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}
implies
{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{1}},x^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}_{1}})\neq\emptyset
, which transfers to

{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}}((I,{\underline{x}}),(I^{\prime},{\underline{x}}^{\prime}))\neq\emptyset

by Lemma
5.2.7
.
This proves
(I,{\underline{x}})\sim_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}(I^{\prime},{\underline{x}}^{\prime})
, as required for injectivity of
|\psi|
.
This map and its inverse are continuous since its lift
\psi
on objects is continuous with continuous local inverses.
This completes the proof of (ii).

To see that
\psi
has the local equivariance stated in (iii), note that since

\psi:{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}(V_{I},V_{I})\to{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(V_{i_{1}},V_{i_{1}})
is given by the formula
(I,I,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\mapsto g_{i_{1}}\circ{\rm id}_{x_{1}}
,
the
G
action on
V_{I}
given by
{\underline{x}}\mapsto{\underline{g}}*{\underline{x}}
maps to the
G_{i_{1}}
action on
V_{i_{1}}\subset U_{i_{1}}
.

It follows that each pair
(V_{I},G_{I})
is a local uniformizer for
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
. Thus
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|
is covered by finitely many uniformizers and hence, by Corollary
3.2.6
, is proper because its realization
|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|\simeq F^{\prime}
is Hausdorff.
Thus (i) also holds.

Finally,
the claims in (iv) about the relation between the groupoids
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
are proved in
Proposition
4.2.4
(iii).
∎

Lemma 5.2.7
.

In the setting of Lemma
5.2.5
, given

\displaystyle\qquad I=(k_{\ell_{1}}=i_{1}<\dots)\;\subset\;K=(k_{1}<\ldots),\quad\mbox{\; and }

\displaystyle{\underline{x}}=(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots)\in U_{I},\ \;{\underline{y}}=(y_{k_{1}},n_{k_{1}k_{2}},\ldots)\in U_{K}\mbox{\; with }\psi_{I}({\underline{x}})=\psi_{K}({\underline{y}})

there is a bijection
{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}}((I,{\underline{x}}),(K,{\underline{y}}))\to{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{1}},y_{k_{1}})
given by

U_{K}\times G_{I}\;\ni\;\bigl({\underline{y}},(g_{i_{1}},\ldots,g_{i_{|I|}})\bigr)\mapsto g_{i_{1}}\circ n_{k_{\ell_{1}-1}k_{\ell_{1}}}^{-1}\circ\ldots\circ n_{k_{1}k_{2}}^{-1},

where
i_{s}=k_{\ell_{s}},1\leq s\leq|I|
.

Proof.

The morphism space with fixed source and target in
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}
is

{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{V}}}((I,{\underline{x}}),(K,{\underline{y}}))=\bigl\{({\underline{y}},{\underline{g}})\,\big|\,{\underline{g}}\in G_{I},{\underline{g}}*{\underline{x}}=\rho_{IK}({\underline{y}})\bigr\},

where
\rho_{IK}({\underline{y}})=(y_{k_{\ell_{1}}},n^{K}_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,n^{K}_{i_{|I|-1}i_{|I|}},y_{k_{\ell_{|I|}}})\in U_{I}
,
in the notation of (
5.2.6
).
This does not capture the morphisms
n_{k_{1}k_{2}},\ldots,n_{k_{\ell_{1}-1}k_{\ell_{1}}}
in
{\underline{y}}\in U_{K}
, which compose to

n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{\ell_{1}}}:=n_{k_{1}k_{2}}\circ\ldots\circ n_{k_{\ell_{1}-1}k_{\ell_{1}}}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(y_{k_{1}},y_{k_{\ell_{1}}}).

Here by assumption we have
51
51

51

Recall from Lemma
5.2.5
that
\psi_{K}:V_{K}\to|{\mathcal{X}}|
is the footprint map, not to be confused with the functor
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
.

\psi_{k_{\ell_{1}}}(y_{k_{\ell_{1}}})=\psi_{K}({\underline{y}})=\psi_{I}({\underline{x}})=\psi_{i_{1}}(x_{i_{1}})\;\in\;|{\mathcal{X}}|,

and since
|\psi_{k_{\ell_{1}}}|:V_{k_{\ell_{1}}}/G_{k_{\ell_{1}}}\to F^{\prime}_{k_{\ell_{1}}}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
is a homeomorphism, this implies
y_{k_{\ell_{1}}}=g_{i_{1}}*x_{i_{1}}
for some
g_{i_{1}}\in G_{i_{1}}
that is unique up to
G_{x_{i_{1}}}={\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{1}},x_{i_{1}})\subset G_{i_{1}}
. Any such choice yields
n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{\ell_{1}}}\circ g_{i_{1}}^{-1}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(y_{k_{1}},x_{i_{1}})
, and taking the inverse in the groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}
yields
g_{i_{1}}\circ(n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{\ell_{1}}})^{-1}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{1}},y_{k_{1}})
, which shows that the given map is well defined.

To show that the map is a bijection, we will show that any
m\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{1}},y_{k_{1}})
has exactly one preimage. More precisely, given
m,{\underline{x}},{\underline{y}}
we will iteratively determine the entries in
{\underline{g}}=(g_{i_{1}},g_{i_{2}},\ldots)\in G_{I}
from the conditions
{\underline{g}}*{\underline{x}}=\rho_{IK}({\underline{y}})
and
g_{i_{1}}\circ(n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{\ell_{1}}})^{-1}=m
.
The iteration starts with
g_{i_{1}}\in G_{i_{1}}
being determined uniquely by

{\rm id}_{x_{i_{1}}}\circ g_{i_{1}}=m\circ n^{K}_{k_{1}k_{\ell_{1}}}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{1}},y_{k_{\ell_{1}}}),\quad\mbox{where }k_{\ell_{1}}=i_{1},

using notation from Remark
5.2.3
.
The properties of the local uniformizer in Definition
3.2.1
then also guarantees the first object in
{\underline{g}}^{-1}*\rho_{IK}({\underline{y}})
to be
g_{i_{1}}^{-1}*y_{k_{\ell_{1}}}=x_{i_{1}}
.
For the iteration step, assume
g_{i_{1}},\ldots,g_{i_{s-1}}
are determined so that the first
(s-1)
objects and
(s-2)
morphisms in
{\underline{g}}^{-1}*\rho_{IK}({\underline{y}})
agree with those given by
{\underline{x}}=(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,m_{i_{|I|-1}i_{|I|}},x_{i_{|I|}})
. Then we see that
g_{s}\in G_{i_{s}}
is uniquely determined by matching
m_{i_{s-1}i_{s}}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{s-1}},x_{i_{s}})
with the corresponding morphism in
{\underline{g}}^{-1}*\rho_{IK}({\underline{y}})
,

\displaystyle m_{i_{s-1}i_{s}}\;=\;g_{i_{s-1}}\circ n^{K}_{i_{s-1}i_{s}}\circ g_{i_{s}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\hskip-36.135pt{\rm id}_{x_{i_{s}}}\circ g_{i_{s}}\;=\;(m_{{i_{s-1}i_{s}}})^{-1}\circ g_{i_{s-1}}\circ n^{K}_{i_{s-1}i_{s}}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{s}},y_{k_{\ell_{s}}}).

This indeed determines
g_{i_{s}}\in G_{i_{s}}
since
x_{i_{s}},y_{k_{\ell_{s}}}\in U_{i_{s}}
and the local uniformizer
(U_{i_{s}},G_{i_{s}})
comes with a bijection
{\Gamma}_{i_{s}}:G_{i_{s}}\times U_{i_{s}}\to{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i_{s}},U_{i_{s}}),(g,x)\mapsto{\rm id}_{x}\circ g
. Finally, the local uniformizer property

(s\times t)\circ{\Gamma}_{i_{s}}:G_{i_{s}}\times U_{i_{s}}\to U_{i_{s}}\times U_{i_{s}},\quad(g,x)\mapsto(x,g*x)

implies matching for the
s
-th object
y_{k_{\ell_{s}}}=t({\rm id}_{x_{i_{s}}}\circ g_{i_{s}})=g_{i_{s}}*x_{i_{s}}
.
∎

Remark 5.2.8
.

Let
C\subset|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|=|{\mathcal{X}}|
be any set such that its topology as a subspace of
|{\mathcal{X}}|
is second countable.
We claim that there is a constant
K
such that each sufficiently small open subset in
C
pulls back to a collection of at most
K
disjoint open sets in
\pi_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{-1}(C)
. This holds because

|X_{\mathcal{V}}|
is covered by the footprints of the finite number of uniformizers
(V_{J},G_{J}),J\subset A,
, and each
|V_{J}|
has this property with
K:=|G_{J}|
.
Hence any countable collection of open subsets of
C
that form a basis for the subspace topology of
C
pulls back to
a countable collection of open subsets of
\pi_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{-1}(C)
that form a
basis for the topology of
\pi_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{-1}(C)
.
Thus the inverse image
\pi_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{-1}(C)
of any second countable subset
C\subset|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|=|{\mathcal{X}}|

is second countable in the subspace topology. Note that this might not hold for the map
X\to|{\mathcal{X}}|
.

{\Diamond}

Remark 5.2.9
.

There is an analogous étale groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}
with objects
\bigsqcup_{I}U_{I}
, where
U_{I}
is as in Lemma
5.2.2
, and whose morphisms are generated by the projections
(\rho_{IJ}:U_{J}\to U_{I})_{I\subset J}

and group actions
G_{J}\times U_{J}\to U_{J}
. However, the full space of morphisms in
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}
is rather complicated to describe explicitly because there are morphisms from
U_{I}
to
U_{J}
whenever
|U_{I\cup J}|=F_{I\cup J}\neq\emptyset.
(See
[
MW3
,
Mc1
]
for a discussion of such categories in the finite dimensional case.) Further, the functor
\psi
extends to an
étale equivalence
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
.
The category
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}
per se plays no role in the current paper.

{\Diamond}

5.3.
The strong bundle
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}

We now explain the structure of the pullback bundle
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}:=\psi^{*}{\mathcal{W}}
in the top row of diagram (
-
‣
2.1.2
) defined in Lemma
3.4.4
.
Since we need to establish the properties of strong bundle and sc-Fredholm section functor, this subsection works explicitly with sc-groupoids and polyfolds.
However, many results extend to general étale proper categories.

Throughout we consider a sc-Fredholm section functor
f:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
of a strong bundle
P:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
over an ep-groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}
with compact zero set
|f^{-1}(0)|
as in Definition
2.1.1
.
The bundle is given by structure maps
(P:W\to X,\mu:{\bf X}{\vphantom{\times}}_{s}{\times}_{P}W\to W)
, where
P:W\to X
is a strong bundle over the M-polyfold of objects
X
, and
\mu
defines the action of the morphisms of
{\mathcal{X}}
on the object space
W
of
{\mathcal{W}}
. The following result describes the structure of
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
and the perturbation theory of
f_{\mathcal{V}}
in detail.

Theorem 5.3.1
.

Let
(U_{i},G_{i})_{i=1,\ldots,N}
be a finite collection of local uniformizers whose footprints
F_{i}:=|U_{i}|
cover
S:=|f^{-1}(0)|\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}F_{i}
, and let
\bigl(F^{\prime}_{I}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|\bigr)_{I\subset\{1,\ldots,N\}}
be a cover reduction as in Lemma
5.2.4
, with corresponding étale data
(V_{J},G_{J},\rho_{IJ})
as in Lemma
5.2.5
.
Then pullback with the étale functor
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
from Theorem
5.2.6
induces a sc-Fredholm section functor
f_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
of a strong bundle
(P_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}},\mu_{\mathcal{V}})
over the reduced ep-groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
, whose perturbation theory is equivalent to that of
f
.
In detail:

(i)

The strong bundle
P_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
is the pullback of

P:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
under
\psi
as in Lemma
3.4.4
.
Using the specific structure from Theorem
5.2.6
, this pullback is given by

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{V}}:

\displaystyle\;\psi^{*}W:=X_{\mathcal{V}}\,{\vphantom{\times}}{}_{\psi}{\times}_{P}W\to X_{\mathcal{V}},\quad P_{\mathcal{V}}(I,{\underline{x}},w)=(I,{\underline{x}}),

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{V}}:

\displaystyle\;{\bf X}_{\mathcal{V}}\,{\vphantom{\times}}{}_{s}{\times}_{P_{\mathcal{V}}}\psi^{*}W\to\psi^{*}W,\quad\mu_{\mathcal{V}}(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}},w)=\bigl(t(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}}),\mu(\psi(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}}),w)\bigr),

where
(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}},w)
is an abbreviation for
\bigl((I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}}),s(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}}),w)\bigr)\in{\bf X}_{\mathcal{V}}\,{\vphantom{\times}}{}_{s}{\times}_{P_{\mathcal{V}}}\psi^{*}W
.
The resulting groupoid
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}=\psi^{*}{\mathcal{W}}
is given by

\displaystyle W_{\mathcal{V}}=\psi^{*}W

\displaystyle=\textstyle\bigsqcup_{I\subset A}W_{I}=\textstyle\bigsqcup_{I\subset A}\bigl\{\bigl(I,{\underline{x}}=(x_{i_{1}},\ldots),w\bigr)\,\big|\,{\underline{x}}\in V_{I},w\in W_{x_{i_{1}}}\bigr\},

\displaystyle{\bf W}_{\mathcal{V}}(W_{I},W_{J})

\displaystyle=\bigl\{\bigl(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}},w\bigr)\,\big|\,(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\in{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}(V_{I},V_{J}),\;w\in W_{\psi(s(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}))}\bigr\}

with the structure maps

(5.3.1)

\displaystyle s(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}},w)

\displaystyle=\bigl(s(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}),w\bigr),\qquad t(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}},w)=\bigl(t(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}),\widehat{\rho}_{IJ}({\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})w\bigr),

\displaystyle(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}},w)

\displaystyle\circ(J,K,{\underline{y}},{\underline{h}},w^{\prime})=\bigl((I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\circ(J,K,{\underline{y}},{\underline{h}}),w\bigr)

\displaystyle{\rm id}_{(I,{\underline{x}},w)}

\displaystyle=({\rm id}_{(I,{\underline{x}})},w),\qquad(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}},w)^{-1}=\bigl((I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})^{-1},\widehat{\rho}_{IJ}({\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})w).

Here
\widehat{\rho}_{IJ}
lifts the morphisms in
{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}}(V_{I},V_{J})
to a family of linear maps between
the fibers of the bundles
W_{I}\to V_{I}
,
W_{J}\to V_{J}
. It is given by

(5.3.2)

\displaystyle\widehat{\rho}_{IJ}({\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})w:=\mu(\psi(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}),w)\;\in\;W_{x_{j_{1}}}\;\quad\mbox{ for }\;\;I\subset J,\;w\in W_{({\underline{g}}^{-1}*\rho_{IJ}({\underline{x}}))_{j_{1}}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\rho}_{IJ}({\underline{g}}^{-1}*{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}^{-1}):=(\widehat{\rho}_{JI}({\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}))^{-1}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\mbox{ for }\;\;J\subset I,

and satisfies the following identity (with
{\underline{v}},{\underline{k}}
determined as in (
4.1.4
))

(5.3.3)

\widehat{\rho}_{JK}({\underline{y}},{\underline{h}})\,\widehat{\rho}_{IJ}({\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})=\;\widehat{\rho}_{IK}\bigl(\rho_{I\vee K}({\underline{v}}),{\underline{k}}({\underline{h}}{\underline{g}})|_{I\wedge K}\bigr).

(ii)

The natural pullback functor
\Psi:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
from Lemma
3.4.4
restricts to a strong bundle equivalence
\Psi:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}|_{V}
in the sense of
[
HWZ
, Def.10.4.1]
, where

V=\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{I\subset A}F^{\prime}_{I}\bigr)=\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}({\rm im\,}|\psi|)\subset X
as in Theorem
5.2.6
.

In the notation of Theorem
5.2.6
, this functor is given by

(I,{\underline{x}},w)\mapsto w
on objects and
(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}},w)\mapsto(\psi(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}}),w)
on morphisms.

(iii)

The sc-Fredholm section functor
f_{\mathcal{V}}:=\psi^{*}f:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
is the pullback of
f
under
\psi
as in Lemma
3.4.4
.
Using the specific structure from Theorem
5.2.6
, this functor is given by

\displaystyle f_{\mathcal{V}}(I,{\underline{x}})

\displaystyle=\bigl(I,{\underline{x}},f(\psi(I,{\underline{x}}))\bigr)=\bigl(I,{\underline{x}},f(x_{i_{1}})\bigr)\quad\text{for}\;(I,{\underline{x}})\in X_{\mathcal{V}},

\displaystyle f_{\mathcal{V}}(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}})

\displaystyle=\bigl(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}},f(\psi(I,{\underline{g}}^{-1}*\rho_{I}({\underline{y}})))\bigr)\qquad\text{for}\;(I,J,{\underline{y}},{\underline{g}})\in{\bf X}_{\mathcal{V}}.

In particular,
\psi
restricts to a functor between the full subcategories
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\supset f_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}(0)\to f^{-1}(0)\subset{\mathcal{X}}
and induces a homeomorphism
|\psi|:|f_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}(0)|\to|f^{-1}(0)|
between the compact zero sets.

The inner action of
G:=\prod_{i\in A}G_{i}
on
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
naturally lifts to an inner action on
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
as in Lemma
3.3.6
, and
f_{\mathcal{V}}
is
G
-equivariant.

If, in addition,
f
is orientable, then so is
f_{\mathcal{V}}
; any choice of orientation of
f
induces an orientation of
f_{\mathcal{V}}
via pullback with
\Psi
as specified in §
2.3
. This definition is such that if
f,f_{\mathcal{V}}
are transverse then
|\psi|:|f_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}(0)|\to|f^{-1}(0)|
is orientation preserving.

(iv)

The perturbation theories of
f
and
f_{\mathcal{V}}
are equivalent as follows.
We can find compactness controlling data
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
for

as in Definition
3.5.1
with
|{\rm cl}_{X}({\mathcal{U}})|\subset|V|
.
Then
(N_{\mathcal{V}}:=N\circ\Psi\,,\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}}:=\psi^{-1}({\mathcal{U}}))
is compactness controlling data for
f_{\mathcal{V}}
and we have a pushforward construction for regular perturbations:

For every
(N_{\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}})
-regular sc
+
-multisection functor
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
in the sense of Definition
3.5.4
the pushforward
\Psi_{*}\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{W}}|_{V}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
extends to an
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
-regular sc
+
-multisection functor
\widetilde{\Lambda}:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
that is trivial
52
52

52

A multisection functor
\Lambda:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
is called trivial at

if

and
\Lambda(w)=0
for all

.

over
X{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{U}}\supset X{\smallsetminus}\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(|V|)
. Moreover,

|\psi|\;:\;\bigl|S_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}}=\{{\underline{x}}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}|\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}(f_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{x}}))>0\}\bigr|\;\to\;\bigl|S_{\widetilde{\Lambda}\circ f}=\{q\in X\,|\,\widetilde{\Lambda}(f(q))>0\}\bigr|

is a bijection between the perturbed zero sets, and the corresponding branched ep
+
-subgroupoids

is the
[

pullback under the equivalence
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}|_{V}
.
If

is oriented, and

is equipped with the orientation from (iii), then the identification

is orientation preserving in the sense that the
[

, Thm.11.3.5]
pullback orientation of the branched ep

-subgroupoid

coincides with the orientation of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}
induced from the orientation of

via the solution set convention in §

.

In the special case of a polyfold model without boundary in the sense of Definition
5.1.2
for a moduli space
{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}\simeq|f^{-1}(0)|
, that is when
\partial{\mathcal{X}}=\emptyset
and
