.

Proof.

Our constructions follow the theory of compatible perturbations in
[
Sc
]
, which simplifies in our setting as
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
is a local sc-diffeomorphism and induces a topological embedding
|\psi|:|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|\to|{\mathcal{X}}|
, thus meets the “topological pullback condition” in
[
Sc
, Def.4.3]
.

Proof of (i):
Lemma
3.4.4
proves the first part of (i):
(P_{\mathcal{V}},\mu_{\mathcal{V}})
is a strong bundle in the sense of
[
HWZ
, Def.8.3.1]
. The structure maps of
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
result from writing out the construction,
using the specific form of
\psi
in Theorem
5.2.6
(ii). To prove (
5.3.3
),
we use the fact that the composite
(m,w)\circ(m^{\prime},\mu(m,w))=(m\circ m^{\prime},w)
has target

(5.3.4)

\mu(m^{\prime},\mu(m,w))=t(m^{\prime},\mu(m,w))=t(m\circ m^{\prime},w)=\mu(m\circ m^{\prime},w).

Since by definition
\widehat{\rho}_{IJ}({\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})w=\mu(\psi(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}),w)
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\rho}_{JK}({\underline{y}},{\underline{h}})\widehat{\rho}_{IJ}({\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})

\displaystyle\;=\;\mu\bigl(\psi(J,K,{\underline{y}},{\underline{h}}),\,\mu(\psi(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}),w)\bigr)

\displaystyle\;=\;\mu(\psi(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}})\circ\psi(J,K,{\underline{y}},{\underline{h}}),w)\;=\;\widehat{\rho}_{IK}\bigl(\rho_{I\vee K}({\underline{v}}),{\underline{k}}({\underline{h}}{\underline{g}})|_{I\wedge K}\bigr),

where the last expression is explained in (
4.1.4
).

Proof of (ii):

The existence of the strong bundle map
\Psi:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
in (ii) is established in Lemma
3.4.4
, and its formula can be deduced from (
3.4.3
). Moreover
\Psi:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}|_{V}
is a strong bundle equivalence by Lemma
3.4.4
(v).

Proof of (iii):

The proof that
f_{\mathcal{V}}
is a sc-Fredholm section functor is given in Lemma
3.5.3
, while Lemma
3.4.4
(v) shows that
|\psi|:|f_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}(0)|\to f^{-1}(0)|
is a homeomorphism.
Next, by Lemma
3.3.6
, the inner action of
G
on
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
lifts to
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
f_{\mathcal{V}}
is
G
-equivariant.
We will denote the induced action on
W_{\mathcal{V}}
by

(5.3.5)

\displaystyle(I,{\underline{x}},w)\mapsto{\underline{g}}*(I,{\underline{x}},w):

\displaystyle=\bigl(I,{\underline{g}}|_{I}*{\underline{x}},\,\mu(\psi(I,I,{\underline{g}}|_{I}*{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}|_{I}),w)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(I,{\underline{g}}|_{I}*{\underline{x}},\,g_{i_{1}}*w\bigr)

where the second equality holds because
\psi(I,I,{\underline{g}}|_{I}*{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}|_{I})=g_{i_{1}}
by Theorem
5.2.6
(ii).
If, in addition,
f
is orientable, then so is
f_{\mathcal{V}}
, and any choice of orientation of
f
induces an orientation of
f_{\mathcal{V}}
via pullback with
\Psi
, since this is a case of the equivalence convention in §
2.3
.
It also is a special case of pushforward
[
HWZ
, Thm.12.5.7]
with the diagram
{\mathcal{W}}|_{V}\overset{\Psi}{\leftarrow}{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\overset{\rm Id}{\rightarrow}{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
which is a generalized strong bundle isomorphism in the sense of
[
HWZ
, Def.10.5.2]
.
Moreover, Remark
2.3.1
shows that if
f,f_{\mathcal{V}}
are transverse then
|\psi|:|f_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}(0)|\to|f^{-1}(0)|
is orientation preserving.

Proof of (iv):

The fact that compactness controlling data
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
for
f
with
|{\rm cl}_{X}({\mathcal{U}})|\subset|V|
exists and pulls back to compactness controlling data
(N_{\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}})
for
f_{\mathcal{V}}
is proved in Lemma
3.5.3
.
Next note that when
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
is a sc
+
-multisection functor
with support in
{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}}
then by Lemma
4.3.5
(i) it may be pushed forward to a multisection
\Lambda:=\Psi_{*}\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{W}}|_{{\widetilde{V}}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
that is defined over the saturation
{\widetilde{V}}:=\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(|V|)
of
V
in
{\mathcal{X}}
and is sc
+
-smooth. by construction; also see

[
HWZ
, Lemma 11.5.2]
. Since it has support in the saturated subset
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\widetilde{V}}
,
it may be smoothly extended by the trivial multisection of
{\mathcal{W}}|_{X{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{U}}}
(that equals
1
along the zero section and is
0
elsewhere) to a sc
+
-multisection of
{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
.

Next, suppose that
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}
is
(N_{\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}})
-regular in the sense of Definition
3.5.4
, that is

(1)

N_{\mathcal{V}}(\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}})=\sup\bigl\{N_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{w}})\,\big|\,{\underline{w}}\in W_{\mathcal{V}}[1],\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{w}})>0\bigr\}\;<\;1
;

(2)

\text{\rm dom-supp}\,\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}={\rm cl}_{X_{\mathcal{V}}}\bigl\{y\in X_{\mathcal{V}}\,\big|\,\exists\,{\underline{w}}\in P_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}(y){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{w}})>0\bigr\}\;\subset\;{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}}=\psi^{-1}({\mathcal{U}})
;

(3)

All local sections representing
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}
are transverse to
f_{\mathcal{V}}
.

We now show that
\Lambda=\Psi_{*}\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}
is an
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
-regular perturbation of
f|_{\widetilde{V}}:{\mathcal{X}}|_{\widetilde{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}|_{\widetilde{V}}
(where as above
{\widetilde{V}}
denotes the saturation of
V
in
{\mathcal{X}}
) because it satisfies the corresponding versions of these conditions.

Proof of (iv)-(1):
We have

\displaystyle 1>N_{\mathcal{V}}(\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}})

\displaystyle=\sup\bigl\{N_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{w}})\,\big|\,{\underline{w}}\in W_{\mathcal{V}}[1],\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{w}})>0\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\sup\bigl\{N(\Psi({\underline{w}}))\,\big|\,{\underline{w}}\in W_{\mathcal{V}}[1],\Lambda(\Psi({\underline{w}}))>0\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\sup\bigl\{N(w)\,\big|\,w\in\Psi(W_{\mathcal{V}}[1]),\Lambda(w)>0\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\sup\bigl\{N(w^{\prime})\,\big|\,w^{\prime}\in W[1],\Lambda(w^{\prime})>0\bigr\}\;=\;N(\Lambda).

Here the last identity uses the fact that
N
is invariant under morphisms and
|{\mathcal{W}}|_{V}|=|\Psi|(|{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}|)
, so every
w^{\prime}\in W
with
|P(w)|\in|V|
is related by a morphism to some
w=\Psi({\underline{w}})
. Moreover, for
w^{\prime}\in W[1]
the corresponding object
{\underline{w}}
in
N(w^{\prime})=N(\Psi({\underline{w}}))
lies in
W_{\mathcal{V}}[1]
since both the morphisms of
{\mathcal{W}}
and the strong bundle map
\Psi
preserves the sc-filtrations.

Proof of (iv)-(2):
Note that the pullback identity
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{o}},w)=\Lambda(\Psi({\underline{o}},w))=\Lambda(w)
identifies

\displaystyle\bigl\{{\underline{o}}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}\,\big|\,\exists({\underline{o}},w)\in P_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}({\underline{o}}){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{o}},w)>0\bigr\}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigl\{{\underline{o}}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}\,\big|\,\exists\,w\in P^{-1}(\psi({\underline{o}})){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda(w)>0\bigr\}

\displaystyle\quad=\psi^{-1}\bigl(\bigl\{o\in X\,\big|\,\exists\,w\in P^{-1}(o){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda(w)>0\bigr\}\bigr).

Now,
\pi_{\mathcal{X}}:{\widetilde{V}}\to|V|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
is continuous and
|\psi|:|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|\to|V|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
is a homeomorphism,
and the operations of closure and realization commute for saturated subsets of étale proper groupoids by Lemma
3.1.8
. Further the set

\bigl\{o\in X\,\big|\,\exists\,w\in P^{-1}(o){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda(w)>0\bigr\}
is saturated because
{\Lambda}
is a functor. Therefore

\displaystyle\bigl|\text{\rm dom-supp}\,\Lambda\bigr|

\displaystyle=\bigl|{\rm cl}_{V}\bigl\{o\in X\,\big|\,\exists\,w\in P^{-1}(o){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda(w)>0\bigr\}\bigr|

\displaystyle={\rm cl}_{|V|}\bigl|\bigl\{o\in X\,\big|\,\exists\,w\in P^{-1}(o){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda(w)>0\bigr\}\bigr|

\displaystyle={\rm cl}_{|V|}\bigl(|\psi|\bigl(\bigl|\bigl\{{\underline{o}}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}\,\big|\,\exists({\underline{o}},w)\in P_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}({\underline{o}}){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{o}},w)>0\bigr\}\bigr|\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=|\psi|\bigl({\rm cl}_{|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|}\bigl(\bigl|\bigl\{{\underline{o}}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}\,\big|\,\exists({\underline{o}},w)\in P_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}({\underline{o}}){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{o}},w)>0\bigr\}\bigr|\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=|\psi|\bigl(\bigl|{\rm cl}_{X_{\mathcal{V}}}\bigl\{{\underline{o}}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}\,\big|\,\exists({\underline{o}},w)\in P_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}({\underline{o}}){\smallsetminus}\{0\}:\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{o}},w)>0\bigr\}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle=|\psi|\bigl(\bigl|\text{\rm dom-supp}\,\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}\bigr|\bigr)\subset|\psi|(|{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}}|)=|{\mathcal{U}}|\subset|{\mathcal{U}}|.

Since
{\mathcal{U}}
is a saturated subset of
\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(|V|)
and the operations of closure and realization commute for saturated subsets of étale proper groupoids by Lemma
3.1.8
, this implies
\text{\rm dom-supp}\,\Lambda\subset{\mathcal{U}}
as required.

Proof of (iv)-(3):

This is formally stated as “
{\rm T}_{(f|_{V},\Lambda)}
is surjective on
{\rm supp\,}(\Lambda\circ f|_{V})
”. To check this, consider any point in the perturbed solution groupoid,
o\in{\widetilde{V}}\subset X
with
(\Lambda\circ f)(o)>0
.
Since
{\rm im\,}|\psi|=|V|
we can find
{\underline{o}}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}
with
|\psi({\underline{o}})|=|o|
and, by construction of the pullback section
f_{\mathcal{V}}=\psi^{*}f
and multisection
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}=\Lambda\circ\Psi
, we have

(\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}})({\underline{o}})=(\Lambda\circ\Psi)({\underline{o}},f(\psi({\underline{o}})))=\Lambda(f(\psi({\underline{o}})))>0.

Now
|\psi({\underline{o}})|=|o|
implies the existence of a morphism
\psi({\underline{o}})\to o
in
{\mathcal{X}}|_{V}
, functoriality of
f
lifts this to a morphism
f(\psi({\underline{o}}))\to f(o)
in
{\mathcal{W}}|_{V}
, and functoriality of
\Lambda
then implies
\Lambda(f(\psi({\underline{o}})))=\Lambda(f(o))>0
.
That means
{\underline{o}}
lies in the perturbed solution set
{\rm supp\,}(\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}})
, so if
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}
is locally represented by sections
({\mathfrak{s}}_{i}:N_{\underline{o}}\to W_{\mathcal{V}}|_{N_{\underline{o}}})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}
, then by assumption
{\rm D}(f_{\mathcal{V}}-{\mathfrak{s}}_{i})({\underline{o}}):{\rm T}_{\underline{o}}X_{\mathcal{V}}\to P_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}({\underline{o}})
is surjective for each
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
with
{\mathfrak{s}}_{i}({\underline{o}})=f_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{o}})
.
Now the corresponding pushforward sections
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{i}(x)=\Psi\bigl({\mathfrak{s}}_{i}(\psi_{{\underline{o}}}^{-1}(x))\bigr)
near
\psi({\underline{o}})
have linearizations

{\rm D}(f-{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{i})(\psi({\underline{o}}))=\Psi\circ({\rm D}(f_{\mathcal{V}}-{\mathfrak{s}}_{i})({\underline{o}}))\circ{\rm d}\psi({\underline{o}})^{-1}.

Since

\Psi:P_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}({\underline{o}})\to P^{-1}(\psi({\underline{o}}))
and
{\rm d}\psi({\underline{o}})
are isomorphisms, these inherit surjectivity from
{\rm D}(f_{\mathcal{V}}-{\mathfrak{s}}_{i})
.

To check that the surjectivity of the linearized sections at perturbed solutions also transfers to the original point
o
, we need to consider the sections

\widetilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{i}:N_{o}\to W|_{N_{o}},x\mapsto\mu\bigl(t_{m}^{-1}(x),{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{i}(s_{\mathcal{X}}(t_{m}^{-1}(x)))\bigr)

obtained by transfer with morphisms near a choice of
m\in s^{-1}(\psi({\underline{o}}))\cap t^{-1}(o)
. To compute their linearization, note the identity
f(x)=\mu\bigl(t_{m}^{-1}(x),f(s_{\mathcal{X}}(t_{m}^{-1}(x)))\bigr)
, which is an expression of the functoriality of
f
as in Definition
3.4.2
.
Since
\mu
is linear in the second factor and we consider only linearizations of sections with

(f-{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{i})(s_{\mathcal{X}}(t_{m}^{-1}(o)))=(f-{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{i})(s_{\mathcal{X}}(m))=(f-{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{i})(\psi({\underline{o}}))=0,

the resulting linearization of

(f-\widetilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{i})(x)=\mu\bigl(t_{m}^{-1}(x),(f-{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{i})(s_{\mathcal{X}}(t_{m}^{-1}(x)))\bigr)

at
x=o
is

{\rm D}(f-\widetilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{i})(o)=\mu(m,\cdot)\circ{\rm D}(f-{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{i})(\psi({\underline{o}}))\circ{\rm d}s_{\mathcal{X}}(m)\circ{\rm d}t_{\mathcal{X}}(m)^{-1}.

Here
{\rm d}s_{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\rm d}t_{\mathcal{X}}
are isomorphisms as source and target maps are local sc-diffeomorphisms, and
\mu(m,\cdot):W_{s(m)}\to W_{t(m)}
is invertible with inverse given by
\mu(m^{-1},\cdot)
,
as can be verified from the algebraic properties of
\mu
in Definition
3.4.1
(iii).

Thus we have shown that
\Lambda=\Psi_{*}\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}
is an
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
-regular perturbation of
f|_{\widetilde{V}}:{\mathcal{X}}|_{\widetilde{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}|_{\widetilde{V}}
(where as above
{\widetilde{V}}:=\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(|V|)
is the saturation of
V
). Since
{\Lambda}
has support in
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\widetilde{V}}
we may extend it smoothly to a multisection
\widetilde{{\Lambda}}
of
{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
by defining
\widetilde{\Lambda}(0_{x})=1,x\in X{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{U}}
, and
\widetilde{\Lambda}(w)=0
for
w\in W|_{X{\smallsetminus}{\mathcal{U}}},w\neq 0
. Since
{\rm supp\,}(\widetilde{\Lambda})={\rm supp\,}({\Lambda})
is unchanged,
\widetilde{\Lambda}
is an
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
-regular perturbation of
f:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
.

If
{\mathcal{X}}
has boundary (and corners), then good or general position of the kernels of linearizations at solutions on the boundary (see
[
HWZ
, Def.3.6.6, 5.3.9]
) transfers from
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}
to
\Lambda
because the kernels are identified via
{\rm d}\psi({\underline{o}})
, which also identifies the reduced tangent spaces of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
{\mathcal{X}}
.

To check that
|\psi|
identifies the perturbed zero sets, note that the construction of
f_{\mathcal{V}}
by pullback and
\widetilde{\Lambda}
by pushforward yields

\Lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}\;=\;\Psi^{*}\widetilde{\Lambda}\circ\psi^{*}f\;=\;\widetilde{\Lambda}\circ f\circ\psi
.
Here
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}|_{V}
is the equivalence of ep-groupoids from Theorem
5.2.6
, so in particular induces a homeomorphism
|\psi|:|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|\to|V|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
. This homeomorphism restricts to a bijection

|\{{\underline{x}}\in X_{\mathcal{V}}\,|\,\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}(f_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{x}}))>0\}|\simeq|\{q\in X\,|\,\widetilde{\Lambda}(f(q))>0\}|

since
\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}(f_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{x}}))>0\Leftrightarrow\widetilde{\Lambda}(f(\psi({\underline{x}})))>0
and
|\{q\in X\,|\,\widetilde{\Lambda}(f(q))>0\}|\subset|V|={\rm im\,}|\psi|
, where the latter inclusion is guaranteed by the facts that the unperturbed zero set
|f^{-1}(0)|
is contained in
|V|
and the perturbation
\widetilde{\Lambda}:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
is trivial over
X{\smallsetminus}{\widetilde{V}}
.

Next, if
f
is oriented then this induces an orientation of the branched ep
+
-subgroupoid
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
by
[
HWZ
, Thm.15.4.3]
– using the solution set convention in §
2.3
.
If
f_{\mathcal{V}}
is equipped with any orientation, then this analogously orients the branched ep
+
-subgroupoid
\widetilde{\Lambda}\circ f:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathbb{Q}}^{\geq 0}
.
Now the claim is that pulling back the orientation of
f
to an orientation of
f_{\mathcal{V}}
as in (iii) and then orienting the perturbed solution set
\Theta_{\mathcal{V}}:=\Lambda_{{\mathcal{V}}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}
by the solution set convention for each local branch has the same result as orienting the perturbed solution set
\Theta:=\widetilde{\Lambda}\circ f
with the solution set convention and then pulling back this orientation to
\Theta_{\mathcal{V}}=\Theta\circ\psi
as in
[
HWZ
, Thm.11.3.5]
.
The latter pullback construction uses the fact that
\psi
restricts to diffeomorphisms on all sufficiently small (finite dimensional) local branches. So the identification of these two orientation constructions can be checked at the level of local branches, where this is done in Remark
2.3.1
.

Finally, we consider the special case
\partial{\mathcal{X}}=\emptyset
with
f
oriented and a homeomorphism
\psi_{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}:|f^{-1}(0)|\to{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}
to a compact Hausdorff space.
Then the composition
\psi_{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}\circ|\psi|:|f_{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}(0)|\to{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}
is also a homeomorphism by (iii), and we can prove equality of the induced fundamental classes
[{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}]_{f_{\mathcal{V}}}=[{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}]_{f}\in\check{H}_{d}({\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}};{\mathbb{Q}})
defined in Theorem
5.1.4
as follows:
Given compactness controlling data
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
, a nested sequence of open subsets
B_{k}\subset B_{k-1}\subset|U|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
such that
\bigcap_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}B_{k}=|f^{-1}(0)|
, we defined the first fundamental class

[{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}]_{f}:={\psi_{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}}*\bigl(\underset{\leftarrow}{\lim}\,(\psi_{k})_{*}[\Lambda_{k}\circ f]\bigr)
, where
\Lambda_{k}
is any choice of
(N,{\mathcal{U}}\cap\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(B_{k}))
-regular perturbations of
f
and
\psi_{k}:|\Lambda_{k}\circ f|\to B_{k}\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
are the corresponding continuous maps from the realization of the branched ep
+
-subgroupoids
\Lambda_{k}\circ f
, defined as
Theorem
5.1.4
.

Since Theorem
5.1.4
shows that the result is independent of the choices, we can choose
{\mathcal{U}}\subset X
with
{\rm cl}_{X}({\mathcal{U}})\subset{\widetilde{V}}:=\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{I}F_{I}^{\prime}\bigr)
so that, as shown above, the pulled back data
(N_{\mathcal{V}},{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}})
controls compactness of
f_{\mathcal{V}}
. Since
|{\mathcal{U}}|\subset|{\widetilde{V}}|=|V|
we can also pull back the nested sequence of open subsets

with the homeomorphism

to obtain

B_{{\mathcal{V}},k}:=|\psi|^{-1}(B_{k})|\subset B_{{\mathcal{V}},k-1}\subset|{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{V}}|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}|

with intersection

.
This data induces the fundamental class

[{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}]_{f_{\mathcal{V}}}:=({\psi_{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}}\circ|\psi|)_{*}\bigl(\underset{\leftarrow}{\lim}\,(\phi_{{\mathcal{V}},k})_{*}[\Lambda_{{\mathcal{V}},k}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}]\bigr),

where

-regular perturbations of

and

are the corresponding continuous maps of the realizations.

Now we can compare the result with the fundamental class
[{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}]_{f}
by choosing
\Lambda_{k}
as the pushforward
\Psi_{*}\Lambda_{{\mathcal{V}},k}
, trivially extended to
X{\smallsetminus}{\widetilde{V}}
as above.
Then we saw above that
|\psi|
restricts to homeomorphisms
\bigl(|S_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}}|,|\Lambda_{\mathcal{V}}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}|\bigr)\simeq\bigl(|S_{\widetilde{\Lambda}\circ f}|,|\widetilde{\Lambda}\circ f|\bigr)
and
B_{{\mathcal{V}},k}\simeq B_{k}
, and hence identifies the corresponding

giving
53

53

Here, as in Remark

and Theorem

, the map

.

|\psi|_{*}(\phi_{{\mathcal{V}},k})_{*}[\Lambda_{{\mathcal{V}},k}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}]=(\phi_{k})_{*}[\Lambda_{k}\circ f]\;\;\in\;\;\check{H}_{d}(B_{k}),

With that we obtain as claimed

\displaystyle[{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}]_{f_{\mathcal{V}}}

\displaystyle=(\psi_{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}})_{*}|\psi|_{*}\bigl(\underset{\leftarrow}{\lim}\,(\phi_{{\mathcal{V}},k})_{*}[\Lambda_{{\mathcal{V}},k}\circ f_{\mathcal{V}}]\bigr)=(\psi_{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}})_{*}\bigl(\underset{\leftarrow}{\lim}(\phi_{k})_{*}[\Lambda_{k}\circ f]\bigr)\;=\;[{\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}}]_{f}.

∎

Remark 5.3.2
.

Given a collection of
U
-data as in Lemma
5.2.2
, one can, as mentioned in Remark
5.2.9
, form a category
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}
analogous to
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
together with an étale equivalence
\psi:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{X}}|_{U}
where
U=\pi^{-1}\bigl(\bigcup_{i}U_{i}\bigr)
.
Similarly, there is a pullback bundle
{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathcal{U}}:{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}
with section
f_{\mathcal{U}}

that satisfies (appropriately modified versions of) the properties
in Theorem
5.3.1
. However, there are too many morphisms in
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}
for
the perturbation theory of multisections of
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{U}}
to be any easier to understand than that of the original bundle
{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
.
On the other hand the perturbation theory of
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
is more transparent.
Indeed, as explained in
as explained in Theorem
5.2.6
(iii),(iv), (and see also §
4
),
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
can be reconstructed from the nonsingular groupoid

{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}
by adding morphisms given by the
G
action. Moreover,
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}
is the completion of the poset
{{\mathcal{Q}}}
, and we will see in Theorem
5.4.2
that, as in Proposition
4.1.6
, we can push forward constant sections of the trivial bundle
{{\mathcal{Q}}}\times E\to{{\mathcal{Q}}}
and then average over the
G
-action to obtain transverse multisections of
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
.

{\Diamond}

5.4.
Fredholm stabilization over reduced ep-groupoids

Let
f:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
be a sc-Fredholm section functor of a strong bundle
(P:W\to X,\mu:{\bf X}{\vphantom{\times}}_{s}{\times}_{P}W\to W)
over an ep-groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}=(X,{\bf X})
with compact solution set
|f^{-1}(0)|\subset|{\mathcal{X}}|
.
Then Theorems
5.2.6
and
5.3.1
construct the right hand square in the diagram (
-
‣
2.1.2
) and establish parts (i)–(iv) of the global stabilization Theorem
2.1.2
.
To complete its proof, it remains to construct the stablization functor
\tau:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
and establish the properties in part (v).
Proposition
4.1.5
showed that to construct such
\tau
we only need to construct a suitable functor
{\tau}
from the trivial bundle
{{\mathcal{Q}}}\times E\to{{\mathcal{Q}}}
to
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
. Before embarking on this construction, we first address the fact that
in order for the corresponding pushed forward multisections
{\Lambda}_{{\mathcal{V}},e}
in Proposition
4.1.6
to be both sc
+
and compactness controlled, we need to put extra conditions on the initial set of local stabilizations used in Lemma
5.2.2
as the basis for the construction of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}

Lemma 5.4.1
.

Given compactness controlling data
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
for
f
as in Definition
3.5.1
there is a finite collection
(U_{i},G_{i})_{i\in A}
of local uniformizers of
{\mathcal{X}}
and
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
-regular Fredholm stabilizations
\tau_{i}:X\times E_{i}\to W[1]
as follows:

\bullet

The domains of the local uniformizers are open subsets
U_{i}\subset X
which cover the zero set
|f^{-1}(0)|\subset\bigcup_{i\in A}|U_{i}|
. The local morphisms over each
U_{i}
are identified with
G_{i}
-actions on
U_{i}
via bijections
{\Gamma}_{i}:G_{i}\times U_{i}\to{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(U_{i},U_{i})
as in Definition
3.2.1
.

\bullet

E_{i}
is a finite rank vector space with a linear action
G_{i}\times E_{i}\to E_{i},(g,e)\mapsto g*e
.

\bullet

\tau_{i}:X\times E_{i}\to W[1]
is sc-smooth (thus sc
+
to
W
), linear in
E_{i}
, and a bundle map; in particular
P(\tau_{i}(x,e))=x
.
Moreover,
\tau_{i}|_{U_{i}\times E_{i}}
is
G_{i}
-equivariant in the sense that
\tau_{i}(g*x,g*e)=\mu({\Gamma}_{i}(g,x),\tau_{i}(x,e))
for all
g\in G_{i},x\in U_{i}
.

\bullet

\tau_{i}
is an
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
-regular stabilization of the Fredholm section
f|_{U_{i}}:{\mathcal{X}}|_{U_{i}}\to{\mathcal{W}}|_{U_{i}}
as follows:

(1)

Each
E_{i}
is equipped with a
G_{i}
-invariant norm
\|\cdot\|_{E_{i}}
such that
N(\tau_{i}(x,e))\leq\|e\|_{E_{i}}
;

(2)

{\rm supp\,}\tau_{i}(\cdot,e)\subset{\mathcal{U}}
for all
e\in E_{i}
;

(3)

for any unperturbed solution
y\in f^{-1}(0)\cap U_{i}
the linearization of
f-\tau_{i}:X\times E_{i}\to W
,
(y,e)\mapsto f(y)-\tau_{i}(y,e)
at
(y,0)
is surjective.
If the solution lies in the boundary
y\in\partial X
, we can moreover achieve general (and hence good) position of
f-\tau_{i}
at
y
as in
[
HWZ
, Def.3.6.6, 5.3.9]
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.2.1
we can find a local uniformizer
(U_{x},G_{x},{\Gamma}_{x})
around each
x\in f^{-1}(0)
, and since
{\mathcal{U}}\subset X
is a saturated neighbourhood of
f^{-1}(0)
, we can shrink their domains to
U_{x}\subset{\mathcal{U}}
.
In addition, the nonlinear sc-Fredholm property
[
HWZ
, Def.3.1.16]
of
f
includes a regularizing property due to which every
x\in f^{-1}(0)
lies in the “smooth part”
X_{\infty}\subset X
described in §
2.3
. Moreover,
[
HWZ
, Thms.3.1.22, 3.1.26]
guarantee that each linearization
{\rm D}f(x):{\rm T}_{x}X\to W_{x}
at a solution is a sc-Fredholm operator in the sense of
[
HWZ
, Def.1.1.9]
.
In fact the restriction to the reduced tangent space
{\rm D}^{R}f(x):={\rm D}f(x)|_{{\rm T}_{x}^{R}X}:{\rm T}_{x}^{R}X\to W_{x}
is sc-Fredholm since
{\rm T}_{x}^{R}X\subset{\rm T}_{x}X
is a sc-subspace – identical when
x
lies in the interior of
X
, or of finite codimension given by the degeneracy index
d(x)\geq 1
when
x\in\partial X
; see
[
HWZ
, Def.2.4.7]
.
The sc-Fredholm operator
{\rm D}^{R}f(x)
in particular has a finite dimensional sc-complement
C_{x}\subset W_{x}
of the image,
W_{x}=C_{x}\oplus{\rm im\,}{\rm D}^{R}f(x)
.
By
[
HWZ
, Prop.1.1.7]
, the notion of a sc-complement guarantees that it lies in the “smooth part” of the fiber, that is in a local trivialization
\Phi:W|_{\text{nbhd}(x)}\to\bigcup_{u\in O}{\rm im\,}{\Gamma}(u)\subset O\times F
as in §
2.2
we have
\Phi(C_{x})\subset F_{\infty}=\bigcap_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}}F_{m}
in the dense subset of smooth points in the sc-Banach space
F=(F_{m})_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}}
.
Now let
w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{x}}\in C_{x}
be a basis, scaled such that
N(w_{l})\leq\frac{1}{2}
for each
l=1,\ldots,d_{x}
.
Then
[
HWZ
, Lemma 5.3.3]
provides sc
+
-sections
{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,1},\ldots,{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,d_{x}}:X\to W[1]
with
{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(x)=w_{l}
,
N({\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(y))\leq 1
for all
y\in X
and
{\rm supp\,}{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}\subset U_{x}
.

Now
E_{x}:=({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{x}})^{G_{x}}\cong\bigoplus_{h\in G_{x}}\{h\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{x}}
is a vector space of dimension
\#G_{x}\cdot d_{x}
with linear
G_{x}
-action defined on generators by
g*(h,\vec{c}):=(gh,\vec{c})
.
Then we construct
\tau_{x}:X\times E_{x}\to W[1]
on generators
(h,\vec{c})\in E_{x}
by

\tau_{x}\bigl(y,\bigl(h,\vec{c}=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{d_{x}})\bigr)\bigr):=\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(h,h^{-1}*y),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(h^{-1}*y)\bigr)

and extend linearly to
E_{x}=\bigl\{e=\sum_{h\in G_{x}}(h,\vec{c}_{h})\,|\,\vec{c}_{h}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{x}}\bigr\}
.
This map
\tau_{x}:X\times E_{x}\to W[1]
is linear in
E_{x}
and satisfies
P(\tau_{x}(y,e))=t({\Gamma}_{x}(h,h^{-1}*y))=h*h^{-1}*y=y
by construction.
It is sc
∞
with values in
W[1]
(thus sc
+
with values in
W
) since the sections
{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}:X\to W
are sc
+
(in particular sc
∞
with values in
W[1]
) and
\mu:{\bf X}{\vphantom{\times}}_{s}{\times}_{P}W\to W
is a strong bundle map (in particular restricts to a sc
∞
-map
{\bf X}{\vphantom{\times}}_{s}{\times}_{P}W[1]\to W[1]
). To check equivariance we use the algebraic properties of
{\Gamma}
in Definition
3.2.1
and
\mu
in Definition
3.4.1
on generators:

\displaystyle\tau_{x}(g*y,(gh,\vec{c}))

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(gh,(gh)^{-1}*g*y),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}((gh)^{-1}*g*y)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(gh,h^{-1}*g^{-1}*g*y),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(h^{-1}*g^{-1}*g*y)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(gh,h^{-1}*y),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(h^{-1}*y)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(h,h^{-1}*y)\circ{\Gamma}_{x}(g,y),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(h^{-1}*y)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(g,y),\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(h,h^{-1}*y),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(h^{-1}*y)\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(g,y),\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(h,h^{-1}*y),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(h^{-1}*y)\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(g,y),\tau_{x}(y,(h,\vec{c}))\bigr).

Towards
(N,{\mathcal{U}})
-regularity note the following properties:

(1)

N(\tau_{x}(y,e=\sum_{h\in G_{x}}(h,\vec{c}_{h})))\leq\sum_{h\in G_{x}}N(\tau_{x}(y,(h,\vec{c}_{h})))\leq\|e\|_{E_{x}}
where we equip
E_{x}
with the norm
\|\sum_{h\in G_{x}}(h,\vec{c}_{h})\|_{E_{x}}:=\sum_{h\in G_{x}}\|\vec{c}_{h}\|_{\ell^{1}}
with
\|(c_{1},\ldots,c_{d})\|_{\ell^{1}}:=|c_{1}|+\ldots+|c_{d}|
. Note that this norm is
G_{x}
-invariant by construction. Then
N(\tau_{x}(y,e))\leq\|e\|_{E_{x}}
follows by summing over the estimates on generators

\displaystyle N(\tau_{x}(y,(h,\vec{c}_{h})))

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}|c_{l}|N\bigl(\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}(h,h^{-1}*y),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(h^{-1}*y)\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}|c_{l}|N\bigl({\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(h^{-1}*y)\bigr)\leq\textstyle\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}|c_{l}|

where we used compatibility of
N
with the bundle morphisms encoded by
\mu
as in
[
HWZ
, Def.12.2.1 (3)]
.

(2)

{\rm supp\,}\tau_{x}(\cdot,e)\subset U_{x}\subset{\mathcal{U}}
for all
e\in E_{x}
since
{\rm supp\,}{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}\subset U_{x}
,
U_{x}
is invariant under
G_{x}
, and
U_{x}\subset{\mathcal{U}}
.

(3)

{\rm D}^{R}(f-\tau_{x})(x,0):{\rm T}_{x}^{R}X\times E_{x}\to W_{x}=C_{x}\oplus{\rm im\,}{\rm D}^{R}f(x)
is surjective since the
derivatives of

\tau_{x}(x,({\rm id},\vec{c}))=\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot\mu\bigl({\Gamma}_{x}({\rm id},x),{\mathfrak{s}}_{x,l}(x)\bigr)=\sum_{l=1}^{d_{x}}c_{l}\cdot w_{l}

in the direction of
\vec{c}
span
C_{x}={\rm span}\{w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{x}}\}
.

Note that (3) implies in particular that
{\rm D}(f-\tau_{x})(x,0):{\rm T}_{x}X\times E_{x}\to W_{x}=C_{x}+{\rm im\,}{\rm D}f(x)
is surjective.
For
x\in\partial X
, we claim that (3) also implies general/good position
[
HWZ
, Def.3.6.6, 5.3.9]
. Towards this, choose a sc-complement of the reduced kernel
{\rm T}_{x}^{R}X=P\oplus\ker{\rm D}^{R}(f-\tau_{x})(x,0)
, then the surjectivity in (3) implies that
{\rm D}(f-\tau_{x})(x,0):P\to W_{x}
is a sc-isomorphism.
As a result,
P
is also a complement of the full kernel,
{\rm T}_{x}X=P\oplus\ker{\rm D}(f-\tau_{x})(x,0)
, which confirms general position as the complement
P
lies in the reduced tangent space. And this is a special case of the complement required by the good position property
[
HWZ
, Def.3.1.24]
for
N=\ker{\rm D}(f-\tau_{x})(x,0)
. Indeed, in a local chart around
x\simeq(0,0)\in[0,\infty)^{d(x)}\times E=:C_{x}
, we have
P\subset{\rm T}^{R}_{x}X\simeq\{0\}\times E
and hence
n\in C_{x}\Leftrightarrow n+p\in C_{x}
for
(n,p)\in N\times P
.

Finally, note that the local uniformizers provide an open cover of the zero set
|f^{-1}(0)|\subset\bigcup_{x\in f^{-1}(0)}|U^{\prime}_{x}|
. Since the latter is compact, there is a finite cover
|f^{-1}(0)|\subset\bigcup_{i\in A}|U_{x_{i}}|
, and we rename all the structures by replacing the subscripts
x_{i}
with
i
in the finite set
A
.
∎

Lemma
5.4.1
provides a cover of the zero set
|f^{-1}(0)|\subset\bigcup_{i\in A}|U_{i}|=:F
, which lifts to a covering
\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(F)=\bigcup_{i\in A}\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(|U_{i}|)
by saturated open sets
\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(|U_{i}|)\subset X
.
Now
[
HWZ
, Thm.5.5.10, Cor.5.5.17, Thm.7.5.7]
, and our assumptions in Definition
2.1.1
guarantee the existence of a sc-smooth partition of unity
\sum_{i\in A}|\beta_{i}|\big|_{F}\equiv 1
subordinate to this saturated open cover in the sense of
[
HWZ
, Def.7.5.5]
. Since the index set
A=\{1,\ldots,N\}
is finite, this notion simplifies to

(5.4.1)

(a)

\displaystyle\qquad{\rm supp\,}\beta_{i}\subset\pi_{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}(|U_{i}|)\mbox{ for }\ i=1,\ldots,N,

(b)

\displaystyle\qquad\mbox{each }\beta_{i}:{\mathcal{X}}\to[0,1]\ \mbox{ is a sc-smooth functor.}

Here the category
[0,1]
has objects
[0,1]
and only identity morphisms.

When constructing the left hand square in the diagram (
-
‣
2.1.2
), we need to choose the cover reduction that defines
f_{\mathcal{V}}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
to be compatible with such a partition of unity.

Theorem 5.4.2
.

Given

-

a finite collection

(U_{i},G_{i},\tau_{i}:X\times E_{i}\to W)_{i\in A}

of local uniformizers and Fredholm stabilizations as in Lemma
5.4.1
,

-

a choice of order
A=\{1,\ldots,N\}
, and

-

a partition of unity

as in (a)-(b) above,

choose

a cover reduction

for

,

and let

-

for

as follows:

(i)

The finite dimensional vector space

.
The étale (but usually not proper) groupoid

from Theorem

There is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The inclusion

The Fredholm stabilization functor

This induces a sc-smooth functor
\tau:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}[1]
.

(iii)

The functor

(iv)

The functor
\tau
is a compactness-controlled Fredholm stabilization in the following sense:
The pullback
(N_{\mathcal{V}}:=N\circ\Psi,\,{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{V}}:=\psi^{-1}({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}))

Moreover, the family of maps
\bigl(\tau_{\underline{e}}:X_{\mathcal{V}}\to W_{\mathcal{V}},{\underline{o}}\mapsto\tau({\underline{o}},{\underline{e}})\bigr)_{{\underline{e}}\in E}
is

-regular in the sense

for all

;

-invariant neighbourhood

of

,
({\underline{o}},{\underline{e}})\mapsto f_{\mathcal{V}}({\underline{o}})-\tau({\underline{o}},{\underline{e}})
is transverse to the zero section of

in the sense that its linearization at every zero is onto.
Moreover, it is in general (hence good) position at zeros in the boundary
\partial X_{\mathcal{V}}\times O_{\tau}
in the sense that the reduced linearizations are onto.

Proof.

Proof of (i):
The groupoid
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E
is well defined and étale
by Lemma
4.1.4
(ii), but, by Remark
4.2.2
(iii), not in general proper.
In particular, its object and morphism spaces inherit an M-polyfold structure from
the object and morphism spaces of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}
in Theorem
5.2.6
(iv)
by Cartesian products with the finite dimensional vector space
E
, which has a unique sc-Banach space structure
(E_{k}=E)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}}
.
In this sc-smooth structure, the structure maps of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E
are local sc-diffeomorphisms because they are products of the structure maps of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}
with the identity.
This proves (i).

Proof of (ii)–(iii):

First recall that the finite dimensional vector space
E
carries a unique sc-structure
(E_{k}=E)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}}
, which in particular coincides with its shift that appears in the notion of strong bundle.
Thus the strong bundle structure of
{\rm pr}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}
only requires
\mu_{\rm pr}:{\rm Mor}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}}\times E\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E
to be linear in
E
and sc-smooth, which
\mu_{\rm pr}(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}},{\underline{e}})=\bigl(t(I,J,{\underline{x}},{\underline{g}}),{\underline{e}}\bigr)
is since the target map
t
is sc-smooth.

The étale functor
{\iota}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}
is the identity on objects and an inclusion on morphisms.

The functor
\tau:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}
in (
2
) is defined on objects
by the formulas in (
5.4.6
).
The summands for
n\geq 2
in this expression can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mu\bigl(m_{i_{n-1}i_{n}}^{-1}\circ\ldots\circ m_{i_{1}i_{2}}^{-1},\beta_{i_{n}}(x_{i_{n}})\tau_{i_{n}}(x_{i_{n}},e_{i_{n}})\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad=\mu(m_{i_{n-1}i_{n}}^{-1})\bigl(\ldots\bigl(\mu(m_{i_{1}i_{2}}^{-1})(\beta_{i_{n}}(x_{i_{n}})\tau_{i_{n}}(x_{i_{n}},e_{i_{n}}))\bigr)\ldots\bigr),

where we denote the fiberwise restrictions of the strong bundle map
\mu:{\bf X}{\vphantom{\times}}_{s}{\times}_{P}W\to W
in Definitions
2.1.1
and
3.4.1
by

(5.4.7)

\mu(m)\,:\;W_{s(m)}\to W_{t(m)},\quad w\mapsto\mu(m,w).

These summands in (
5.4.6
) are then well defined in
W_{x_{i_{1}}}[1]
because we have

\beta_{i_{n}}(x_{i_{n}})\in[0,1],\quad\tau_{i_{n}}(x_{i_{n}},e_{i_{n}})\in W_{x_{i_{n}}}[1],\quad m_{i_{k}i_{k+1}}\in{\rm Mor}_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{i_{k}},x_{i_{k+1}})

so that
\mu(m_{i_{k}i_{k+1}}^{-1}):W_{x_{i_{k+1}}}[1]\to W_{x_{i_{k}}}[1]
is a linear map between the appropriate fibers of
W\to X
, preserving the shifted sc-structure.
Moreover, each of these summands is linear in
e_{i_{n}}
since
\tau_{i_{n}}
is linear on fibers and each
\mu(m^{-1})
in (
5.4.7
) is linear. This shows that
\tau
on objects is well defined and linear in
E=E_{1}\times\ldots\times E_{N}
. Note also that each
\tau_{I}
is trivial (i.e. the zero map) in the factors
E_{j}
for
j\notin I
. (For a more precise claim see (
5.4.9
) below.)

To check that
\tau
is a strong bundle map in the sense of Remark
2.1.1
and Definition
3.4.2
it remains to show that it is sc-smooth with respect to both sc-structures on
W
(thus in particular
\tau:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{V}}^{{\smallsetminus}G}\times E\to{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{V}}[1]
should be sc-smooth), and also is compatible with morphisms.

Towards the former, note that the first summand in (
5.4.6
),
\beta_{i_{1}}(x_{i_{1}})\tau_{i_{1}}(x_{i_{1}},e_{i_{1}})\in E
varies sc-smoothly with

\bigl({\underline{x}}=(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,m_{i_{|I|-1}i_{|I|}},x_{i_{|I|}})\,,\,{\underline{e}}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{N})\bigr)\in V_{I}\times E

since by construction of the sc-smooth structure on
V_{I}\subset U_{I}
in Lemma
5.2.2
the projection
U_{I}\ni{\underline{x}}=(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,)\mapsto x_{i_{1}}\subset X
is a local sc-diffeomorphism, and
\tau_{i_{1}}
is sc-smooth as map to
W[1]
by construction in Lemma
5.4.1
, and hence also sc-smooth after composition with the inclusion
W[1]\to W
.
The further summands

\bigl({\underline{x}}\,,\,{\underline{e}}\ \bigr)\mapsto\mu(m_{i_{n-1}i_{n}}^{-1})\ldots\mu(m_{i_{1}i_{2}}^{-1})\beta_{i_{n}}(x_{i_{n}})\tau_{i_{n}}(x_{i_{n}},e_{i_{n}})

are sc-smooth to
W[1]
and hence
W
, since
\tau_{i_{n}}
has this property by construction, the maps in (
5.4.7
) are strong bundle maps, and each of the maps

\displaystyle V_{I}

\displaystyle\to X,\qquad{\underline{x}}=(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,m_{i_{|I|-1}i_{|I|}},x_{i_{|I|}})\mapsto x_{i_{n}}

\displaystyle V_{I}

\displaystyle\to{\bf X},\qquad{\underline{x}}=(x_{i_{1}},m_{i_{1}i_{2}},\ldots,m_{i_{|I|-1}i_{|I|}},x_{i_{|I|}})\mapsto m_{i_{\ell-1}i_{\ell}}^{-1}

is sc-smooth as follows: In a chart for
U_{I}\supset V_{I}
these maps are represented by
x_{i_{1}}\mapsto x_{i_{n}}
resp.
x_{i_{1}}\mapsto m_{i_{\ell-1}i_{\ell}}
, which can be expressed as composition of target maps, local inverses of source maps, and inversion — all of which are sc-smooth since they are restrictions of the étale structure maps of
{\mathcal{X}}
.

To see that
\tau
is compatible with morphisms, it suffices to check that it satisfies the conditions in
Proposition
4.1.5
, namely

(a)

it is compatible with the morphisms in the poset
{{\mathcal{Q}}}\times E
;

(b)

the induced map on object spaces is
G
